# Branch 11 — Production Readiness: Outstanding Tasks

Single source of truth for Branch 11 (eBay/AliExpress/DSers dropshipping automation) work still outstanding. Replaces scattered tracking across `STATUS.md` (infra-troubleshooting doc — nothing from there is carried forward as of 2026-08-30, per Travis) and `branch11-listing-rules.md`'s old "Next steps" list (still the detailed historical/architecture reference for anything below — this doc is the punch list, that one is the full record).

Worked automatically by a cron job every 30 minutes (`branch11-production-readiness`), and reviewed with Travis in chat periodically.

## How this document is worked (the cron's operating contract)

Each run:

1. **Pick the next open item below**, in list order (open tasks are ordered by priority — see "How this list is prioritized" below, not by item number). Skip anything already `NEEDS DECISION` with no new information to add — those wait on Travis, don't re-research them every run unless something changed.
2. **Research it for real** — read the referenced code/files, re-check live state (e.g. DSers's actual current MCP tool catalog, not a remembered summary) rather than trusting this document's notes as still-current.
3. **Decide: implement, or ask?**
   - If it's a product/business/architecture call only Travis can make — **do not implement it**. Write the research findings and concrete options/tradeoffs under that item, mark it `NEEDS DECISION (updated <timestamp>)`, and stop touching that item this run.
   - If it's a clear engineering task with no real judgment call needed — **implement it**: write/edit code, test it, verify the behavior actually works, then mark it `DONE (<timestamp>)` with a short summary of what was built and how it was verified.
   - When genuinely unsure which bucket an item falls in, default to `NEEDS DECISION` — asking costs a chat message, guessing wrong on a production system doesn't undo itself.
4. **Hard Constraints (below) govern every run, no exceptions inferred from context.** In particular: never take a real-world irreversible action (placing a real supplier order, sending a real customer-facing message, creating/editing/pricing a real eBay listing beyond the existing narrow auto-list carve-out, or using a transaction-capable credential beyond an already-established, tested, code-level pattern) without that already being an explicit approved carve-out documented below or in `branch11-listing-rules.md`.
5. **Log a one-line entry in the Run Log** at the bottom: timestamp, item worked, outcome (`implemented` / `needs decision` / `blocked` / `no-op — nothing actionable`).
6. If every open item is `NEEDS DECISION` and nothing new was learned, do nothing else — a logged no-op run is a correct outcome, not a failure to find work.
7. **Once an item reaches `DONE`, move its full write-up from "Open tasks" down into "Completed"** (bottom of this doc) rather than leaving it inline — keeps the open list scannable. Don't shorten the write-up when moving it; full detail stays for traceability.

## How this list is prioritized (reorganized 2026-08-31, Travis's explicit request)

Ordered by real impact against the overarching goal: automate as much as possible of the full funnel — quality items discovered → listed on eBay → linked in DSers → **fulfilled, tracked, and managed** as real orders. Four tiers:

- **Tier 1 — funnel gaps that directly cap sales/scale.** Either a hard bottleneck (order fulfillment is still 100% manual — Travis is the ceiling on every single sale) or a hard cap on listing volume (genuinely multi-variant products are skipped entirely, regardless of match/profit quality). Also includes the one piece of infrastructure that turns this whole list from "happens when Travis asks in chat" into "happens automatically."
- **Tier 2 — listing success rate.** More of what gets discovered actually lists correctly (right category, no silent systemic blockers) — doesn't grow the candidate pool, but stops losing candidates that were already good.
- **Tier 3 — catalog quality on what's already listed.** Richer descriptions/variant data/dimensions improve conversion and reduce disputes on existing listings — real, but a multiplier on existing sales, not a source of new ones.
- **Tier 4 — margin protection / safety, lower urgency right now.** Real value, but time-sensitive urgency has dropped (see each item's note) or is blocked on preconditions not yet met.

## Hard constraints (carried from `branch11-listing-rules.md`, do not weaken without Travis's explicit say-so)

- Never place a real supplier order autonomously (real money moving).
- Never send a real customer-facing email/message autonomously until a step has a demonstrated, reviewed track record and Travis explicitly approves going live on it.
- Never create/edit/price a real eBay listing autonomously — stays propose/approve, **except** the narrow existing carve-out: the top `AUTO_LIST_TOP_N` (default 5) most profitable HIGH-verdict, profit-passing candidates from each daily run auto-publish to production. Does not extend to MEDIUM-verdict matches, Steps 8-11, or any listing edit/price-change after the fact.
- eBay seller API, AliExpress account, DSers account, and any other transaction-capable credential are a materially higher-stakes secret class — never provision/use without Travis's explicit go-ahead for that specific use, checked each time, not assumed from a prior approval.
- This whole pipeline stays research/design/prototype-only until Travis explicitly says otherwise, stage by stage.

## Open tasks

Item numbers preserved from `branch11-listing-rules.md` for traceability.

### Tier 1 — funnel gaps that directly cap sales/scale

0. **Infrastructure, not a numbered item — register the 30-min `branch11-production-readiness` cron.** `REGISTERED (2026-09-01, via chat, Travis)` — all 4 Branch 11 crons are now registered and verified correct; see the "All 4 crons registered" note under Item 22 below for the full verification pass. Command payload crons default to `delivery.mode: "announce"` (fallback-deliver final text to a chat) unless `--no-deliver` is passed — an isolated cron session has no "last" chat to route to, so `openclaw cron list` correctly flagged it as "will fail-closed" on delivery, which (per `--best-effort-deliver`'s own help text: "do not fail the job if delivery fails") would have marked otherwise-successful runs as failed. Fixed live via `openclaw cron edit cd51d531-6a6e-4962-8c3d-7cca3f20ea95 --no-deliver`, confirmed `delivery.mode` is now `"none"` — matching the two other already-working Branch 11 crons exactly (`branch11-daily-item-research`, `branch11-daily-order-report`, both `delivery.mode: "none"`, both `lastRunStatus: "ok"`). The command below is corrected with `--no-deliver` for any future re-registration; the remaining 3 pending cron commands elsewhere in this doc already include it.
    ```bash
    openclaw cron add "branch11-production-readiness" \
      --every 30m \
      --command "flock -n /tmp/branch11-production-readiness.lock /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting/branch11_production_readiness_run.sh" \
      --command-cwd /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting \
      --description "Branch 11: works the production-readiness backlog every 30 min -- research, implement, or flag for decision, per PRODUCTION-READINESS.md" \
      --session isolated \
      --no-deliver \
      --timeout-seconds 1700
    ```
    Cost note (unchanged from when first raised): real `claude -p` calls, up to 48/day, bounded to one turn per run — genuine ongoing API cost/rate-limit exposure, different risk shape than the now-zero-LLM daily pipeline.
    - **New real bug found and fixed, 2026-09-12 (cron-driven production-readiness run): 3 of the 4 Branch 11 crons' registered `--command` strings had a literal embedded newline splitting `flock -n <lockfile>` from the wrapper script path into two separate shell statements**, silently defeating each cron's own self-overlap lock on every single run. Found while investigating a real, recurring `stderr: flock: bad file descriptor: '/tmp/branch11-price-check.lock'` line in `branch11-price-check`'s run diagnostics (present in 10/11 retained runs, not a one-off). **Root-caused, not just patched**: `openclaw cron get <id>`'s raw `payload.argv` showed the exact string `"flock -n /tmp/branch11-price-check.lock\n  /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting/branch11_price_check_run.sh"` — a real `\n`, not a space or line-continuation. Reproduced live: `sh -lc "flock -n /tmp/test.lock\n  echo hello"` prints the identical `bad file descriptor` error *and* still runs `echo hello` — because the newline splits it into `flock -n /tmp/test.lock` (invalid usage with no command, fails harmlessly) followed by a completely separate, **unprotected** second statement. Same class of bug as the terminal-line-wrap issue already documented above and in Item 22's registration history, but manifesting differently (a broken lock, not a dropped flag) and never previously diagnosed. **Real exposure confirmed live**: the exact same defect was present in `branch11-production-readiness` (this cron itself, every 30 min) and `branch11-dsers-mapping` (every 30 min) — both had run this entire time with no working self-overlap protection. `branch11-enrichment`'s command was already correctly single-line (fixed once already for a different reason, see Item 28's registration note); `branch11-daily-item-research`/`branch11-daily-order-report` were never flock-wrapped to begin with (once-daily jobs, lower overlap risk by design, not a regression). **Fixed**: `openclaw cron edit <id> --command "flock -n /tmp/<job>.lock /home/.../<script>.sh"` (single line, no newline) for all 3 affected crons (`df3804e0-...` price-check, `cd51d531-...` production-readiness, `01a07041-...` dsers-mapping) — verified live via `cron get` immediately after each edit that `payload.argv`'s command string is now a clean single line. This is a pure infra/registration fix (correcting a cron's own command string to match what this doc already documented as the intended command all along) — no Hard Constraint touched, no eBay/DSers/AliExpress write involved. **Real residual risk this closes**: without a working self-overlap lock, a slow run overlapping the next scheduled tick of the same cron could have caused concurrent writes to the same ledger/doc files (e.g., two `branch11-production-readiness` sessions editing this very document at once) — no evidence this ever actually happened (all observed runs completed well under their 30-min interval), but the protection is now real rather than silently absent. **Verified live, next tick**: `branch11-dsers-mapping`'s first scheduled run after the fix (26 min later) completed with `status: ok` and no `flock: bad file descriptor` line anywhere in its diagnostics — the fix holds in production, not just in the `cron get` payload check.

1. **Item 30 — order-processing automation (Steps 9-11), the single biggest end-to-end gap.** `IN PROGRESS`, first concrete piece `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed)`. **Why Tier 1**: right now, every real sale requires Travis to manually place the AliExpress order, wait for tracking, and manually mark the eBay order shipped — this is the actual ceiling on how many orders this business can handle, and it's the most direct match to Travis's own stated goal ("help with order fulfillment, tracking, management"). Pursue automating through **DSers** (2026-08-30 direction, via chat), not direct AliExpress browser automation.
    - **Researched live 2026-08-31, all three real DSers surfaces**: (a) the MCP tool catalog (13 tools, checked live) still has no order-placement/fulfillment tool; (b) DSers's Partner/Open API program is real (confirmed by reading their actual registration page) but is explicitly for third-party developers building apps for *other* merchants (suppliers/platforms/sales agents), not a single merchant automating their own account — needs an app-review approval process (10+ business days) — wrong audience, ruled out; (c) DSers's own native bulk "Place Order" feature (their own how-to guide) auto-fills addresses/variants but **still requires a human to manually complete payment on AliExpress's checkout page** — not a Branch 11 limitation, that's how DSers works for everyone, and it's consistent with (not a workaround for) this project's Hard Constraint that a real supplier order can never be placed autonomously. The same feature **does** auto-sync tracking numbers and mark orders fulfilled once the supplier ships, with zero human action needed for that part.
    - **Real missing link found**: DSers's auto-order/auto-tracking features only work against a **real order object in the store DSers is connected to** (the WooCommerce bridge). Step 8 (`branch11_orders.py`, already live) read real eBay orders and prepped sourcing info for the evening email, but never created anything in WooCommerce — so even with DSers handling the payment-click and tracking-sync, there was nothing for DSers to act on.
    - **Implemented and shipped**: `branch11_orders.py` now mirrors each fully-sourced pending eBay order into the WooCommerce bridge store (`branch11_woocommerce.create_order()`, status `"pending"`, no payment implied) — the exact missing link. New `push_order_to_woocommerce()`, idempotent via a new ledger (`branch11_orders_pushed_to_woo.json`, keyed by eBay order_id, persisted immediately on push) so a still-pending eBay order doesn't get re-pushed as a duplicate WooCommerce order on every run. Only pushes an order if **every** line item is both sourced (`ali_url` on file) and mirrored (`woo_product_id` on file, set at listing time by `_mirror_to_woocommerce()`) — a partial push would create an order in DSers missing part of what the buyer actually ordered, worse than not pushing at all. Fails closed, never raises, never breaks the rest of the (already-working) order report.
    - **Verified**: an isolated monkeypatched test covered fully-sourced push, dedup-on-repeat, partial-sourcing skip, missing-woo-mirror skip, and a WooCommerce-error case — all passed. Then a real live end-to-end test: pushed a real order (using the real "Portable Metal Small Dog Playpen" SKU/ledger data, a synthetic buyer address since no real sale exists yet) to the real WooCommerce API, confirmed via `get_order` that the shipping address and line item (correct product, correct SKU, correct quantity) came through exactly right, then deleted the test order and its ledger entry to leave the bridge store and ledger clean for real use.
    - **Email report updated**: `branch11_orders_run.sh`'s prompt now reports `woo_push` status per order and gives Travis the accurate action ("go to DSers and use Place Order" instead of "order this on AliExpress yourself"), with explicit framing that nothing is ordered/paid for automatically.
    - **What's left** (not started): Travis's own one-click DSers "Place Order" approval is unchanged/manual by design (Hard Constraint). Step 10 (tracking capture) should need no new building — DSers already auto-syncs tracking into the WooCommerce order once the supplier ships — but that auto-sync itself hasn't been observed live yet (no real order has gone through). Step 11 (mark eBay shipped) still needs building: read the tracking number DSers wrote into the WooCommerce order (`branch11_woocommerce.get_order`/`list_orders`, already available) and call eBay's `POST /sell/fulfillment/v1/order/{orderId}/shipping_fulfillment`. Not attempted yet — needs a real order to go through DSers first to confirm the tracking-sync shape before wiring Step 11 against it.
    - **Follow-up, same day (Travis-directed): increased Step 5 (DSers supplier-mapping) frequency.** Live testing above surfaced that a newly auto-listed item became mappable in DSers well before the next day's 8am cron would have reached it — whether from Travis re-clicking "Import Products from WooCommerce" or some periodic sync DSers's own WooCommerce plugin does on its own is still unconfirmed, but either way, mapping shouldn't wait up to 24 hours once a product is actually visible in DSers. **Implemented**: new `branch11_dsers_mapping_run.sh` (bare wrapper around the already-existing `branch11_dsers_mapping.py`, zero LLM cost) — deliberately its own dedicated cron, not folded into the 30-min `branch11-production-readiness` backlog cron, since that cron is an LLM-driven session for working the backlog document, not a place for routine deterministic pipeline steps to run inline (same reasoning as Item 28's daily enrichment cron already being separate). **Verified live**: ran the wrapper directly, confirmed it correctly found 0 newly-eligible items (everything already mapped) and exited clean — safe to run every 30 minutes indefinitely. **Still needs Travis to register the cron** (same registration blocker as the other two crons):
      ```bash
      openclaw cron add "branch11-dsers-mapping" \
        --every 30m \
        --command "flock -n /tmp/branch11-dsers-mapping.lock /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting/branch11_dsers_mapping_run.sh" \
        --command-cwd /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting \
        --description "Branch 11: completes DSers supplier-mapping for newly-visible products every 30 min, so items are order-ready faster than the once-daily 8am cron alone" \
        --session isolated \
        --no-deliver \
        --timeout-seconds 300
      ```

    - **First real order ever, confirmed live 2026-09-19 17:00**: `branch11_orders_pending_2026-09-19.json` has a real entry for the first time in this whole project's history — order `14-15180-44631`, "Makita DJR189... Recipro Saw" (`branch11-395766041202`), $71.96, buyer in Sebastopol, CA. Sourcing status `FOUND`, and `branch11_orders.py` correctly pushed it to WooCommerce (`woo_order_id: 55`) and sent Travis the report email as designed. **Cross-checked against Item 42 immediately given this exact SKU's history** (it was the one real out-of-stock case Item 42's step 1 ever found, back on 2026-09-01, never remapped) — ran a live stock check (see Item 42's new bullet above) and confirmed the supplier is back in stock as of today, so this real order is genuinely fulfillable. Step 10/11 (tracking capture / mark-shipped) remain unbuilt and now have their first real chance to matter — worth watching what DSers's tracking-sync shape actually looks like once Travis places this order, to inform building Step 11 for real instead of guessing.

2. **Item 42 — DSers supplier out-of-stock detection: auto-remap to a new AliExpress supplier, or remove the eBay listing if none is found.** `IN PROGRESS` — step 1 (detection) `DONE (2026-09-01, cron-driven production-readiness run)`, step 2 (remap) `BUILT, propose-only pending decision (2026-09-01, cron-driven production-readiness run)`, step 3 (removal) not started, `NEEDS DECISION`. Ranked Tier 1 per Travis's explicit request. **Why Tier 1**: this is a fulfillment-integrity gap, not just a volume/quality one — if a DSers-mapped item's supplier goes out of stock and nothing changes, the eBay listing stays live and sellable, so a customer could order something Branch 11 can no longer source, risking a failed/refunded order, negative feedback, and account-health exposure. Same category of problem as Item 30 (a real gap on the fulfillment side of the funnel), not a discovery/listing-volume issue.
    - **Step 1 (detect out-of-stock) — DONE, confirmed live 2026-09-01.** Researched both candidate tools directly against the real DSers MCP (fresh `list_tools` schema pull, not assumed): `dsers_inventory_policy_get` turned out to be a red herring — it takes no per-product arguments at all (empty input schema) and returns store-level auto-sync *policy settings* (e.g. "on stock-out, do nothing / no notification"), not a live per-product stock read. The real signal is a `supplier_status` filter parameter on `dsers_my_products` itself: multi-select enum `out_of_stock` (supplier SKU has 0 inventory), `product_out_of_stock` (supplier marked the whole product unavailable), `not_found` (supplier listing gone entirely) — plus `sku_changed`/`cost_changed`, deliberately excluded here since those are Item 22's territory (price/cost drift), not a fulfillment-blocking signal. Confirmed this is a genuine server-side filter, not a guess: individual product records returned by `dsers_my_products` have no visible stock field of their own (`status` stays `"OnSelling"` regardless) — the filter is the only way to see this. One call covers the whole DSers-bridge store (store_id-scoped, and that store only ever contains Branch 11's own mirrored products), no need to loop per-SKU.
      - **Real live finding, not just a mechanism test**: the very first real query surfaced one genuinely affected, currently-live eBay listing — `branch11-395766041202` ("Makita XRJ01Z-R 18V LXT Compact Recipro Saw (Tool Only) Certified Refurbished", listingId `800587340245`, $79.96, listed 2026-08-30), flagged `product_out_of_stock`. This is real: a customer could buy this today and Branch 11 cannot currently source it.
      - **Implemented**: new `branch11_stock_check.py` (standalone DSers session, zero LLM, same pattern as `branch11_price_check.py`/`branch11_dsers_mapping.py`) — queries `dsers_my_products(store_id, supplier_status=[out_of_stock, product_out_of_stock, not_found])`, cross-references the returned `dsers_product_id`s against `branch11_auto_listed.json`'s `dsers_mapped` entries, and emails a plain report (newly flagged / still unresolved / resolved-since-last-check sections) — explicitly states nothing was changed automatically. New ledger `branch11_stock_check.json` (`first_detected_at`/`last_checked_at`/`resolved_at` per SKU) so a still-broken item isn't reported as brand-new every day, and a previously-resolved-then-re-broken item correctly resets as a fresh occurrence rather than staying silently "resolved." Pure read-only against DSers/the ledger — never calls `dsers_sku_remap` or any eBay write endpoint, so it doesn't touch the Hard Constraints at all.
      - **Verified live end-to-end**: real dry-run and real (non-dry) run both correctly found and reported the Makita item; ledger persisted correctly; re-running confirmed dedup (`0 newly flagged, 1 still unresolved`) instead of re-alerting on an already-known issue. Resolved-path logic (an item disappearing from the blocked set gets `resolved_at` set and reported under "resolved since last check") verified with an isolated test that mocked the DSers response to simulate the supplier restocking.
      - **Real testing mistake, disclosed**: that isolated resolved-path test imported the real module (pointing at the real ledger file) and wasn't run with `--dry-run`, so it wrote a false `resolved_at` onto the still-genuinely-broken Makita entry. Caught immediately — the real DSers state was re-checked live (still flagged, confirmed still broken), and the ledger was hand-corrected back to `resolved_at: null` without sending a duplicate email (the correct real state was already known from the live re-check, no need to re-trigger send_email). No real-world listing/order state was ever affected; the mistake was confined to this brand-new tracking ledger, on its first day of existing.
      - New wrapper `branch11_stock_check_run.sh` (same `set -euo pipefail` / cwd / `uv run` pattern as every other daily wrapper), syntax-checked (`bash -n`). **Cron still needs Travis to register**:
        ```bash
        openclaw cron add "branch11-stock-check" \
          --cron "30 13 * * *" \
          --command "flock -n /tmp/branch11-stock-check.lock /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting/branch11_stock_check_run.sh" \
          --command-cwd /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting \
          --description "Branch 11: daily DSers supplier out-of-stock detection email -- flags DSers-mapped live eBay listings whose AliExpress supplier is out of stock/unavailable/gone, detect-and-report only, never auto-remaps or touches the listing" \
          --session isolated \
          --no-deliver \
          --timeout-seconds 300
        ```
        Scheduled 1:30 PM Central, 30 minutes after Item 22's price-check cron (1 PM) — same DSers-session-per-day budget consideration, staggered rather than concurrent.
    - **Step 2 (search + verify + remap) — built and verified 2026-09-01, but deliberately shipped in propose-only mode pending a real decision (see below), not fully autonomous.** New `branch11_stock_remap.py`: for every SKU `branch11_stock_check.json` still shows unresolved, calls `dsers_find_product(keyword=ebay_title, supplier="aliexpress")`, excludes any candidate whose AliExpress id (parsed from `import_url`) matches the already-broken supplier (so a stale/cached search can't "remap" right back to the same dead supplier), then runs `build_dsers_ali_match(..., verify=True)` — the *exact* same rubric/function `--stage finish` already uses to decide what gets auto-listed (construction-material/brand/accessory/wired-wireless mismatch checks, plus the one real browser product-page visit), wrapped in `browser_start()`/`browser_stop()` same as `main()` does. Requires verdict `HIGH` (never `MEDIUM`, matching the Hard Constraint text for the existing eBay auto-list carve-out even though this action isn't an eBay write) AND the replacement must still clear the *original* target margin via `compute_profit()` AND `dsers_sku_remap(mode='preview')`'s diff confidences must all clear 70 (Item 37's threshold) before `mode='apply'` is ever called — never skips the preview step. On a real apply, mirrors the new `ali_url`/`ali_id`/`ali_price`/`ali_shipping_cost` back onto `branch11_auto_listed.json` (so Item 30's WooCommerce order-push and Item 22's price-check see the new supplier, not the dead one) and records a `remap_history` entry. Never touches `branch11_stock_check.json`'s `resolved_at` itself or anything on eBay. New ledger `branch11_stock_remap.json` records every attempt (date, result, candidate, verdict, confidences) whether or not it applied.
      - **Verified**: an isolated mocked test confirmed all three guard branches fire correctly (broken-supplier-id exclusion → `NO_REPLACEMENT_FOUND`, sub-threshold preview confidence → `LOW_CONFIDENCE`, a verified match that fails the margin check → `UNPROFITABLE`).
      - **Real live test against the one real broken SKU (`branch11-395766041202`, the Makita Recipro Saw from step 1) surfaced a genuine, important judgment question, not just a mechanism test.** `dsers_find_product` found a real candidate — "Makita DJR189 18V LXT Brushless Cordless Reciprocating Saw... (Tool Only)" — that cleared every gate: `judge_match()` verdict HIGH (same brand, same 18V, same "tool only" category, no material/accessory/brand mismatch), profit still passes at $79.96, and `dsers_sku_remap(mode='preview')` reported confidence 100. **But it is a different exact model number than the one actually advertised on the live eBay listing** (DJR189 vs. the listed XRJ01Z-R) — same brand/voltage/practical category, not the same SKU. `judge_match()`'s HIGH-verdict bar was built and already trusted for *fresh listing-time* matching, where any sufficiently-close AliExpress source is fine because the eBay listing is built from it — reusing that same bar to swap the supplier behind an **already-existing, already-advertised** listing is a real escalation Travis hasn't explicitly blessed: a customer could pay for "XRJ01Z-R" and receive a box/unit labeled DJR189. That's a materially different risk than a fresh-listing match quality question.
      - **Deliberately did NOT apply this real remap.** Confirmed via a real dry-run (which now also sends the real report email — see next bullet) rather than the destructive `mode='apply'` path; re-checked `branch11_auto_listed.json` afterward to confirm the ledger entry is untouched (`ali_id` still the original broken `1005012963059846`).
      - **Real bug found and fixed during this same testing**: the script's dry-run path originally skipped `send_email()` entirely (only printed to stdout) — harmless when run interactively, but a silent no-op under an unattended cron (stdout is lost). Fixed so the report email always sends regardless of `--dry-run`; only the actual `dsers_sku_remap(mode='apply')` call and ledger writes are gated on it. Re-verified live: the dry-run run now correctly sent a real email subject-tagged `(DRY RUN -- nothing applied)`.
      - New wrapper `branch11_stock_remap_run.sh`, syntax-checked, **deliberately hardcodes `--dry-run`** (propose-only, emails what it *would* do, applies nothing) until the model-exactness question below is resolved — same "propose only until a track record exists" pattern already used elsewhere in this doc for an open decision. **Cron can be safely registered now** since dry-run mode never mutates anything (DSers, the ledger, or eBay):
        ```bash
        openclaw cron add "branch11-stock-remap" \
          --cron "40 13 * * *" \
          --command "flock -n /tmp/branch11-stock-remap.lock /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting/branch11_stock_remap_run.sh" \
          --command-cwd /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting \
          --description "Branch 11: proposes (does not apply) DSers supplier remaps for out-of-stock SKUs flagged by the stock-check email -- dry-run only until Travis decides how strict the same-item bar needs to be for remapping an existing listing" \
          --session isolated \
          --no-deliver \
          --timeout-seconds 600
        ```
        Scheduled 1:40 PM Central, 10 minutes after Item 42 step 1's stock-check cron (1:30 PM) so it has that run's fresh data.
    - **NEEDS DECISION (raised 2026-09-01, real example in hand — not hypothetical)**: is `judge_match()`'s existing HIGH-verdict bar (same brand/category/practical spec, not necessarily the same exact model/SKU) an acceptable bar for autonomously remapping the supplier behind an *already-live, already-advertised* eBay listing, or does remapping need a stricter bar (e.g. also requiring the specific model-number token, when the eBay title has one, to appear in the candidate title) given a customer could receive a differently-labeled item than what they paid for? Real concrete case to decide against: `branch11-395766041202` ("Makita XRJ01Z-R...") vs. the found candidate ("Makita DJR189..."). Recommended default, consistent with how every other auto-execution carve-out in this pipeline was earned: keep `branch11_stock_remap.py` in propose-only (`--dry-run`) mode — surfacing real candidates by email for Travis's manual go-ahead — until either (a) Travis decides the existing bar is fine as-is for this use too, or (b) a stricter model-number-aware bar is built and given its own track record.
    - **Step 3 (remove the eBay listing if no replacement found) — still not started, unchanged.** Same shape as Item 41's open decision — auto-remove vs. propose-only. Recommended default is still propose-only.
    - **Ran the built-but-never-scheduled stock check live, 2026-09-19, prompted by real urgency: today's very first-ever real order (see Item 30) is for `branch11-395766041202` (Makita saw), the exact SKU flagged `product_out_of_stock` back on 2026-09-01 — and the monitoring cron to catch this kind of thing has sat unregistered for 18 days, so the ledger was 18 days stale at the moment it mattered most.** Ran `uv run branch11_stock_check.py --dry-run` first to see current live state without side effects, then a real (non-dry) run since this is the exact detect-and-email-only mechanism it was built for (no eBay/DSers write, never remaps/removes, email goes to Travis not the customer — doesn't touch the Hard Constraints). **Good news, confirmed live**: the Makita supplier is back in stock — `branch11-395766041202` now shows `resolved_at: 2026-09-19` — so today's real order is actually sourceable, it was a real scare, not a real problem. **New concern, also confirmed live**: 5 *other* currently-live listings are newly flagged out-of-stock right now (`branch11-388183263465` Wallet Tracker $28.99, `branch11-385826161635` eufy Doorbell $54.99, `branch11-165439481792` Avolusion 1TB PS5 drive $58.99, `branch11-154939337833` Avolusion 1TB Xbox drive $58.99, `branch11-297246021382` Vena iPad Pro case $17.99) — a real report email was sent to Travis with all 5. Ledger (`branch11_stock_check.json`) is now current as of today. **The real gap this exposes**: this detection mechanism only protects the business if it runs regularly, and it's been sitting built-but-unregistered since 2026-09-01 — the first real order landing on exactly the one SKU this system already knew was broken (before it happened to resolve itself) is a concrete illustration of why. Registering `branch11-stock-check`/`branch11-stock-remap` (commands already given above) is still Travis's call per this doc's established pattern, but flagging plainly: leaving it unregistered means blind spots like this are only caught when a cron-driven run happens to think to check, not reliably.

3. **Item 25 — eBay Variations API support** for genuinely multi-variant items. `IN PROGRESS (2026-09-04, via chat, Travis-directed)` — decision made: option (a) below, scoped to `Color`/`Number of Shelves` real yield (~2/day), not (b)/(c). Phase 3's mechanism is now built and verified live in sandbox; production wiring is the remaining piece, gated on Travis's sign-off per the Hard Constraint (see new bullet below). **Why Tier 1, re-ranked up from "low priority" 2026-08-31**: this is a direct, measurable cap on daily listing volume, not a quality nice-to-have — every candidate whose category needs a genuine variation aspect (Size, Color, etc. per eBay's `aspectEnabledForVariations`) gets excluded by `select_auto_list_candidates()` entirely, regardless of match quality or profit, which is confirmed (per `branch11-listing-rules.md` item 25) as the real cause of many days' auto-listed count landing under `AUTO_LIST_TOP_N`. More listable candidates found doesn't matter if a real chunk of them can never be listed at all. Travis's prior standing decision was to keep skipping these short-term — this re-ranks *when* to build real support, not whether to keep skipping meanwhile.
    - **Supplier-side data source already identified** (found 2026-08-29): `dsers_product_import`/`dsers_product_preview` return real structured per-variant data (an `options` array — `{name, values[]}`, e.g. `Color: [Red, Blue]`, `Size: [S, M, L]` — plus per-variant sell/cost/qty/images). This is the exact ingredient currently missing; today's browser-scraping captures only one title/price/shipping/description per item, no variant structure. Needs the DSers MCP tools (an LLM-driven step, same constraint as the DSers-mapping automation — bare `branch11_pipeline.py` can't call these directly).
    - **Scope note**: Item 28 (Tier 3 below) says it "folds in" this item, but that's imprecise — Item 28's daily enrichment cron can supply the enriched variant/option data, but actually *publishing* a multi-SKU eBay listing via the Variations API is separate, larger engineering work this item still owns. Don't treat Item 28 as a substitute for this one.
    - **Real eBay mechanics confirmed live 2026-08-31** (their developer docs): per-variant `PUT /sell/inventory/v1/inventory_item/{sku}` (already have this) for each variant, each with its own variant-specific aspects → `PUT /sell/inventory/v1/inventory_item_group/{key}` (NEW — `variantSKUs` + `variesBy.specifications`, the pivoting aspect(s) and all covered values; member SKUs must already exist as inventory items) → per-variant `POST /sell/inventory/v1/offer` (already have this) → `POST /sell/inventory/v1/offer/publish_by_inventory_item_group` (NEW — `{"inventoryItemGroupKey": ...}`, publishes every offer in the group together as one multi-variant listing, returns one `listingId`).
    - **Re-scoped 2026-08-31 after research, three separate pieces, not one build** (Travis-directed — goal is quality/profitable items, not raw volume, so precision matters more than speed here): **Phase 1 (DONE, see below)** — most `needs_unresolvable_variation` blocks turned out not to be genuine multi-variant products at all. **Phase 2 = Item 26** (dimension-only blocks — real physical dimensions, not variant choice). **Phase 3 = the actual remaining Variations API build**, now confirmed smaller in scope than originally assumed — recommend it ship with *more* caution than the existing single-SKU auto-list carve-out (e.g. chat-approved per-candidate initially, same trust model as `manual_aspects`) given a wrong variant is a worse customer experience than a skip. Not started.
    - **Phase 1 — DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed).** Live investigation found most blocked candidates aren't genuinely multi-variant — they're single-value cases where eBay's own enum value is itself multi-word/slash-compound (e.g. `Type` = `"Laptop Stand/Riser"` for a broad leaf category like "Stands, Holders & Car Mounts") and the real AliExpress source describes the same concept in different word order or with words in between ("...Wooden Laptop Stand - Ergonomic...Compact Bamboo Desktop Riser...") — `_value_present_in_text()`'s exact-consecutive-phrase requirement correctly finds nothing there even though the concept is obviously present. **Fix**: new `_value_words_scattered_present()` in `branch11_ebay_listing.py` — same evidence bar (every significant word of the value must literally appear, never right after a compatibility qualifier) but doesn't require the words consecutive/in-order. Guards against short/generic-word false positives (only counts words length ≥3). Wired into `_resolve_aspect_value()` as a new tier right after the exact-phrase tier, only accepted if it uniquely identifies exactly one of eBay's values (this tier is looser than exact-phrase matching, so ambiguity is a more realistic risk — same caution already used for the Item 38 synonym tier). **Verified against a full week of real historical data**: of 74 unique candidates previously blocked by `needs_unresolvable_variation`, **20 (27%) now resolve correctly** — spot-checked all 20, all plausible (`Laptop Stand/Riser`, `Desk Stand`, `Storage Hooks`, `Wall Lamp`), no false positives found. The remaining 54 split cleanly: dimension-only blocks (Item Length/Width/Height — Item 26's territory, ~29 each) and genuine remaining variant cases (`Color`: 5, `Number of Shelves`: 4, `Battery Included`: 2, plus 32 `Type` cases where even scattered-word matching found no unique match — correctly still unresolved rather than guessed). Since this tier only ever fires when the existing exact-phrase tier already failed, it cannot regress anything that was already resolving correctly — zero regression risk by construction.
    - **Phase 3 research, 2026-09-01: the real yield is much smaller than assumed, a genuine finding not a hypothetical, before any Phase 3 code was written.** Before building the actual Variations API publish mechanism, checked what's really still blocked: pulled aggregate `unresolvable_variation_aspects` across every available real `branch11_candidates_*.json` (not just today's 2 rows, a full multi-day sample). Non-dimension blocks (Item 26's territory already excluded): **`Type` 53, `Item Length`/`Width`/`Height` ~29 each (Item 26), `Color` 5, `Number of Shelves` 4, `Battery Included` 2, `MPN` 1** — `Type` alone is 53/65 (82%) of everything not already covered by Item 26.
      - **Checked live whether Phase 3's own stated data source (`dsers_product_import`'s `options` array) actually backs the dominant `Type` case, rather than assuming the premise still holds**: pulled real `options` data for 8 real currently/recently-blocked `Type` candidates (a projector screen, laptop stands, garage organizers, a closet system, a macrame curtain, etc.) via `dsers_product_import` (with `dsers_product_delete` cleanup after each, same pattern `branch11_enrichment.py` already uses). **Result: 0/8 have a `Type` option group at all** — DSers only ever offered `Color`/`Size`/`Ships From` for these real items, never `Type`. This makes sense once seen: eBay's `Type` aspect for these categories is a single required descriptive classification (e.g. "Laptop Stand/Riser," "Wall Mount Bracket") that eBay *allows* to be used as a variation-pivot dimension in some categories, not a real customer-facing choice the sourced AliExpress item actually offers multiple values of. DSers's `options` array is about genuine SKU-level choices (a shopper picking Color or Size) — a structurally different thing than eBay's `Type` enum. **Building the Variations API publish mechanism would not resolve any of these 53 `Type` cases** — there's no real multi-value data behind them to build a group listing from, at any confidence level; they're correctly stuck, not blocked by a code gap.
      - **Real remaining yield, quantified**: only `Color` (5) and `Number of Shelves` (4) — 9 real candidates across the whole sampled period (roughly 4-5 real days of runs, so on the order of ~2 candidates/day) — are cases where DSers genuinely does carry multi-value option data Phase 3 could act on. This is a materially smaller number than the "every candidate whose category needs a genuine variation aspect... gets excluded" framing this item was re-ranked to Tier 1 on 2026-08-31 assumed, since that framing (written before this deeper check) implicitly treated all `needs_unresolvable_variation` cases, `Type` included, as Phase-3-fixable.
    - **Decided 2026-09-04 (via chat, Travis-directed): option (a)** — build Phase 3 scoped to the real `Color`/`Number of Shelves` yield (~2/day), not (b)/(c).
    - **Phase 3 mechanism built and verified live in sandbox, 2026-09-04.** New wrappers in `branch11_ebay_listing.py`: `create_inventory_item_group()`, `get_inventory_item_group()`, `delete_inventory_item_group()`, `publish_offer_by_inventory_item_group()`, `withdraw_offer_by_inventory_item_group()` — the two genuinely new eBay calls this phase needed (per-variant `create_inventory_item`/`create_offer` already existed and are reused unchanged, one call per variant value).
      - **Real schema gap found and fixed via the live test, not from docs alone**: eBay's `createOrReplaceInventoryItemGroup` does NOT inherit each member SKU's own non-varying required aspects (Brand/Department/Size Type/etc.) for the group's own publish-time validation — the first real attempt failed `publish_offer_by_inventory_item_group()` with `errorId 25002: "item specific Size Type is missing"` even though every member inventory item already had it set. Added a `common_aspects` parameter to `create_inventory_item_group()` (every required aspect except the pivot, same values already computed for the member items) — confirmed live this closes the gap.
      - **Verified end-to-end against real eBay sandbox** (not mocked): created 2 real variant inventory items (Color: Red/Blue) + 2 real offers, grouped them, published via `publish_offer_by_inventory_item_group()` — got back a real `listingId` (`110590543328`) for one genuine multi-variant listing — then verified the group's real `variantSKUs`/`variesBy` via `get_inventory_item_group()`, and fully tore down (withdraw + delete group + delete both items), confirmed gone via a follow-up `get_inventory_item_group()` returning 404. `py_compile` clean on both changed files.
      - **Credential-architecture prerequisite, also resolved 2026-09-04**: sandbox eBay work had been Bitwarden-only by original design ("no unattended need"), which blocked running this test without Travis present for every iteration. Extended the already-accepted unattended-local-credential pattern (previously production-only) to sandbox too — `branch11_ebay_auth.py`'s `load_local_credentials()`/`refresh_access_token_unattended()` now accept `env="sandbox"`, reading a new `ebay.sandbox` entry in `branch11_unattended_creds.json` (mode 600), bootstrapped once the same way production originally was (Travis unlocked Bitwarden, approved the specific one-time copy of the "eBay Sandbox API" item's fields). Sandbox carries zero real money/inventory risk, so this is strictly lower-stakes than the already-running production path, not a new category of risk. `ensure_test_policies()`/`create_merchant_location()` gained the same `credential_mode` parameter every other function in this chain already had, closing the last gap that would have forced Bitwarden for sandbox testing.
    - **Detection wiring built and verified live, 2026-09-04.** New `branch11_variation_check.py` (standalone `uv run --script`, owns its own asyncio bridge and `mcp`/`httpx2` deps — `branch11_pipeline.py` runs under plain `python3`, which has neither, same reason `branch11_dsers_search.py` is its own script rather than an import): `check_phase3_opportunity()` takes a blocked candidate's category + AliExpress URL + its list of unresolvable variation aspects, and returns a resolved variant plan only if **every** unresolvable aspect is a Phase-3-eligible one (`Color`/`Number of Shelves`, exactly one at a time — this build is single-pivot-aspect only) AND `resolve_variant_values()` finds real, evidence-backed eBay values for at least 2 of DSers's real option values. Fails closed on any error (a DSers call failure, a missing option group, anything) — "no opportunity found," never guessed. Fast-rejects (no DSers call at all, confirmed ~1s) the vast majority of blocked candidates whose unresolvable aspects include Type/dimensions/anything else Phase 3 can't touch, keeping the extra DSers-API-call cost to the rare (~1-2/day at most) case that could plausibly qualify.
      - **Wired into `branch11_pipeline.py`'s `run_auto_listing()`** (new `check_phase3_variant_opportunity()`, a `subprocess.run(["uv", "run", "branch11_variation_check.py", ...])` bridge): right at the existing `needs_unresolvable_variation` block, before giving up. On a genuine opportunity, the candidate is NOT auto-listed — it's flagged with a new distinct `PHASE3_OPPORTUNITY:` `listing_status` and the full resolved plan is stored on the row (`phase3_variant_plan`), never falling through to `list_candidate()`'s real production write path. This is detection-and-flag only, matching the Hard Constraint and this item's own "chat-approved per-candidate, not autonomous" recommendation — the actual multi-SKU publish call chain (reusing the sandbox-verified `create_inventory_item_group()`/`publish_offer_by_inventory_item_group()` wrappers) still needs to be triggered by a live, explicit go-ahead on a specific real candidate, not built to fire on its own.
      - **Email report updated** (`branch11_email_report.py`): a new `=== PHASE 3 OPPORTUNITY ===` section, distinct from the generic failed/skipped bucket, shows the resolved aspect + values + profit breakdown + an explicit "reply/tell Claude which SKU(s) to publish" instruction — so a real opportunity is never silently buried among ordinary skips.
      - **Verified live**: the full `python3 → subprocess uv run → real production eBay API + real DSers session → JSON back` bridge tested end-to-end against a real candidate (correctly returned no opportunity, since that item is Type/dimension-blocked, not Color/Shelves); the fast-reject path timed at ~1s with zero DSers calls for non-eligible aspects; the email section verified with a synthetic opportunity renders correctly. `py_compile` clean on every changed file (`branch11_pipeline.py`, `branch11_variation_check.py`, `branch11_email_report.py`, `branch11_ebay_listing.py`).
      - **Real, honest limitation, not glossed over**: searched every available candidates file (today's run plus all historical daily files) for a genuinely clean Color-only or Number-of-Shelves-only blocked candidate — found none. Every real occurrence co-occurs with a Type or dimension block Phase 3 doesn't resolve. This wiring is ready and will fire the moment a real qualifying candidate appears in a future day's discovery; none has yet.
    - **What's left**: the actual live production multi-SKU publish, the first time a real `PHASE3_OPPORTUNITY` is flagged — chat-approved on that specific candidate, reusing the already-sandbox-verified wrapper functions against PRODUCTION credentials, with real live verification before this path is ever left to run unattended.

48. **Item 48 — real lead on *why* Item 47 recurs (~9 days after Item 45's concurrent-refresh-race fix, with no race this time): this pipeline's DSers integration may be pointed at a legacy/narrower MCP endpoint, not DSers's current documented one.** `NEEDS DECISION, real urgency raised 2026-09-21 — recurred again, this time in ~2.5 days, not ~9`. Raised via chat 2026-09-19, prompted by Travis's question — if the pipeline is already generating DSers activity constantly, why does the refresh token keep dying roughly weekly? Fair question: the code already refreshes proactively every ~6h (the access token's real `expires_in: 21600`) with rotation-safe atomic writes and cross-process locking (Item 45's fix, confirmed still holding — `lsof` showed no lock contention this time), so recurrence genuinely isn't explained by disuse or a repeat of the old race.
    - **Third occurrence confirmed live, 2026-09-21 13:16**: the refresh token has died again — `branch11-dsers-mapping` failing every tick since 12:49 (`error (4x)` and climbing, `Token refresh failed: 400` → `=== DSers one-time authorization required ===`, identical shape to every prior occurrence) and 1pm `branch11-price-check` hit the same wall (ran the full `CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_S=600` before dying). Token file (`branch11_dsers_mcp_tokens.json`) confirms: last successful proactive refresh was 06:14:26 (access token `expires_in: 21600` = 6h, so it expired ~12:14), and the very next refresh attempt (~12:49) got a `400` on the *refresh_token* itself, not just an expired access token — a real dead credential again, not a transient blip. **This is materially faster than the last gap**: the 2026-09-19 09:07:15 re-auth lasted only ~2.5 days before dying, versus ~9 days between Item 45 (2026-08-31) and Item 47 (2026-09-13) — the interval is shrinking, not staying roughly weekly as Travis's original question assumed. This strengthens the case for actually investigating root cause (the legacy-endpoint lead above, or some other real explanation) rather than treating this as a routine weekly chore — at this rate the outage-day fraction of the month is climbing. **Operational impact, same remedy as before**: needs Travis's one-time browser re-auth (same flow as Item 45/47) to restore `branch11-dsers-mapping`/`branch11-price-check`/`branch11-enrichment`/`branch11-daily-item-research`. Not implemented — this is exactly the credential action the Hard Constraints reserve for Travis. Not re-opening Item 47 (its write-up and fix were correct and real) — logging the recurrence here since Item 48 is the live item already tracking this pattern.
    - **What was checked**: DSers's own current MCP documentation (`github.com/dsers/dsers-mcp-server`, `docs/auth.md`) states the "Remote Endpoint" is `https://ai.dsers.com/mcp` — a different domain from what this pipeline actually authenticates against, `https://mcp.dsers.com/dropshipping/mcp` (`branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py`'s `DSERS_MCP_URL`). Live-fetched both domains' OAuth metadata to compare directly, not just going by the docs:
      - `mcp.dsers.com` (ours): `resource_name: "DSers MCP Product Import"`, one scope (`mcp`), served by `Caddy` behind CloudFront.
      - `ai.dsers.com` (current docs): 20 granular scopes (`order:read`, `billing:read`, `product:mapping`, `staff:write`, etc.), served by `istio-envoy` with `x-dsers-request-id` headers — DSers's real internal service mesh, and the one their current docs point integrators to.
    - **Why this matters for Item 47**: a narrower endpoint that current docs don't even mention is exactly the kind of thing run with less operational rigor — shorter/unpublished refresh-token retention, backend restarts against a smaller/in-memory token store, cleanup tied to an unannounced deprecation timeline, etc. No public source (DSers's repo, `auth.md`, `SECURITY.md`, or general MCP-OAuth bug trackers/issues) publishes an actual refresh-token TTL for either endpoint, so this can't be confirmed from outside — but it fits the evidence (dies cold after 9 days of flawless proactive refreshing) far better than anything on our side, since our side is already provably doing everything right.
    - **Not yet known / would need confirming before acting**: whether `ai.dsers.com/mcp` actually supports this pipeline's specific WooCommerce-bridge-via-proxy-store flow — its tool names/shape may differ entirely from `mcp.dsers.com`'s `dsers_store_discover`/`dsers_sku_remap`/etc. `mcp.dsers.com`'s narrow "Product Import" scoping might be *why* this pipeline ended up there in the first place (2026-08-29's original integration), not necessarily an accident.
    - **Recommendation, not yet actioned — two independent tracks**:
      1. **Low-risk, worth doing regardless of root cause**: wrap `dsers_session()` in an outer `asyncio.wait_for()` well under each cron's own job timeout (already flagged under Item 47) so the next dead-token day fails in well under a minute with the alert firing immediately, instead of burning the full 600s `CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_S`. Note the alert email itself already fires correctly and promptly (confirmed: 2026-09-13 13:56:35, ~2s after the dead token surfaced) — this run's 6-day gap was Travis not seeing/acting on the email, not a detection gap, so a faster code failure alone won't close that; worth pairing with a louder channel (push notification/SMS) if a multi-day blind spot is unacceptable.
      2. **Bigger, real decision — evaluate migrating to `ai.dsers.com/mcp`**: only worth pursuing if a real check first confirms it supports this pipeline's store-mapping flow. This is exactly the class of thing the Hard Constraints require Travis's explicit go-ahead for (a new OAuth grant/scope set against his real DSers account) — not something to prototype against production credentials without him deciding first. If he wants it explored, next step is a **read-only** probe under a first-time consent he authorizes, no writes.
    - **Deliberately not done**: no code changes, no new OAuth grant attempted, no `ai.dsers.com` calls made — pure documentation/metadata research only, per the credential Hard Constraint.

### Tier 2 — listing success rate (more of what's found actually lists)

*(no open items currently — Items 32 and 27 both reached `DONE`, moved to Completed below)*

### Tier 3 — catalog quality on what's already listed (conversion/dispute-reduction, not new volume)

### Tier 4 — margin protection / safety, lower urgency right now

4. **Item 41 — eBay Advertising (Promoted Listings) API integration: monitor high-margin items and add/remove them from ad campaigns.** **Add half `DONE` (MVP, 2026-09-02, via chat, Travis-directed)** — removal/per-item-rate logic explicitly deferred, Travis's own framing ("this is mvp... add more logic later"). Builds directly on Item 22's placeholder advertising-candidates section (top-5-by-margin list, "not yet actionable, mechanism TBD") — Travis wants that turned into real logic. Scope, per Travis's request:
    1. **Investigate eBay's real API surface** for managing Promoted Listings campaign membership — what operations actually exist (list campaigns, add/remove a listing to/from a campaign, read campaign-level ad rate and performance), what OAuth scope(s) they need beyond what's already provisioned for the Inventory/Fulfillment APIs this pipeline already uses, and whether membership changes are synchronous or queued/eventually-consistent (relevant given Item 32's precedent — eBay APIs in this account have already shown at least one real eventual-consistency race).
    2. **Design basic, explainable add/remove logic** in the same style as the rest of this pipeline (curated thresholds, no LLM judgment call) — built on Item 22's existing per-SKU margin data (`branch11_price_check.json`) rather than a new data source, so it's monitoring the same numbers Travis already sees in the price-check email.
    3. **Build a monitoring mechanism** that checks current real ad-campaign membership against the desired state and surfaces (at minimum) or acts on (pending the decision below) any difference.
    - **Research done 2026-09-01 (real API docs, via `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` against eBay's actual live developer documentation, not assumed):**
      - **Confirmed real endpoints exist for every piece of scope item 1 asked about**: `POST /sell/marketing/v1/ad_campaign` (`createCampaign`) creates either a **key-based** campaign (seller manually adds specific listings — the shape this item needs) or a **rules-based** campaign (auto-selects listings by criteria like brand — not a fit for per-SKU margin logic); `POST /sell/marketing/v1/ad_campaign/{campaign_id}/ad` (`createAdByListingId`, body: `listingId` + `bidPercentage`) adds one of Branch 11's real listings (the `listing_id` values already sitting in `branch11_auto_listed.json`) to a campaign; `DELETE /sell/marketing/v1/ad_campaign/{campaign_id}/ad/{ad_id}` (`deleteAd`) removes one — but **only for the Cost Per Sale (CPS) funding model**; the alternate Cost Per Click (CPC) model uses a different call (`bulkUpdateAdsStatusByListingId`, setting status `ARCHIVED`) instead of a real delete — which funding model a real campaign on this account would use isn't knowable without actually creating one, a live decision point once building starts, not assumable now. `getAd`/`getAds`/`getAdsByInventoryReference` read current membership (`adId`/`bidPercentage`/`listingId`). Campaign-level ad-rate/performance reporting is real but async, not a simple GET — a `createReportTask`/`getReportTask`/`getReportTasks` pattern (create a report request, poll for completion, then read results), rate-limited to 200 calls/hour for these specific methods (a real, documented constraint — generous for a once-daily check, but relevant if this were ever polled more often).
      - **Real, concrete, previously-unknown finding: reading campaign membership and writing to it are gated by two separate OAuth scopes** — `sell.marketing.readonly` (read: `getAd`/`getAds`/etc.) vs. the full `sell.marketing` (write: `createAdByListingId`/`deleteAd`/etc.). **Neither is currently granted on this account** — confirmed against `branch11_ebay_auth.py`/this project's own re-consent history (production's actual granted scope today is `api_scope` + `sell.account.readonly` + `sell.fulfillment.readonly` + `sell.inventory`, per the 2026-08-27 re-consent record; no Marketing API scope has ever been requested). This means even the read-only "surface current campaign state in an email" half of this item needs a fresh Travis re-consent before any code can be tested live — same checkpoint pattern as every previous scope expansion this project has done, not a rubber stamp.
      - **Eventual-consistency question — genuinely unresolved, not found in eBay's own docs.** Unlike `publish_offer`'s error 25604 (Item 32), which eBay's own error text hinted at, nothing in the Marketing API's public reference documentation states whether a fresh `createAdByListingId`/`deleteAd` call is immediately reflected in a subsequent `getAd` read. Can't respond to Item 32's precedent with a real answer yet — would need to be observed empirically against a real test campaign, the same way Item 32's race was actually found (live, not from documentation).
      - **No prior Promoted Listings use anywhere in this project's history** — confirmed via a full grep of `branch11-listing-rules.md` (zero hits for "promoted listing"/"sell.marketing"/"ad campaign"). This is genuinely greenfield: no existing campaign to read, no existing bid strategy to match, no prior account-level Promoted Listings configuration to account for.
    - **Refined recommendation, now more concrete than the original placeholder**: given the read scope (`sell.marketing.readonly`) and write scope (`sell.marketing`) are separate, real, independently-grantable OAuth scopes — recommend Travis approve **only the read-only scope first**, enough to build and verify the monitoring/reporting half (step 3) end-to-end against his real live campaign state (if any exists in Seller Hub already) before requesting the full write scope needed for any add/remove automation at all. This is a strictly lower-stakes, easier ask than the original "provision Marketing API access" framing, and matches this project's own established pattern of earning trust incrementally (same shape as the DSers-mapping/auto-list track record Items 18/19/25/32/35/36/38/39 built before the existing `AUTO_LIST_TOP_N` carve-out was granted).
    - **Read-only half implemented and shipped 2026-09-02 (via chat, Travis-directed) — exactly the incremental path this item recommended.** Travis's actual ask was narrower than full monitoring: exclude any item already in an active ad campaign from the advertising-candidates section of the price-check email, so it stops suggesting items Travis has nothing new to do with. **Travis re-consented live**: `sell.marketing.readonly` added on top of (not replacing) the 4 existing granted scopes — verified via `branch11_ebay_auth.py status production` before *and* after, confirming no scope was dropped; both the Bitwarden-backed and unattended-local-file credential paths were synced and both verified live with a real token refresh. **Real, previously-unknown finding**: this account already has a real live ad campaign — `"General - 09/01/2026, 15:39"` (campaignId `165304014018`, `RUNNING`, `COST_PER_SALE`, dynamic ad rate capped 10%) — with 3 real listings already enrolled (`800587299263`, `800587299651`, `800581278884`), most likely eBay's own default auto-enrollment rather than anything Travis or this pipeline set up (no prior Promoted Listings use anywhere in this project's history, confirmed 2026-09-01). This makes the filter immediately useful, not speculative: of the 12 currently DSers-mapped/tracked items, 2 (`branch11-406994372466`, `branch11-126395364078`) were already ad-enrolled and would have kept being suggested every day without this fix. **Implemented** in `branch11_price_check.py`: new `_get_advertised_listing_ids()` walks `GET /sell/marketing/v1/ad_campaign` then each campaign's `.../ad` (paginated, not assuming today's small counts hold), using the same unattended local-credential path (`refresh_access_token_unattended`) as auto-listing since this runs from an unattended cron. `build_email_body()` now excludes any tracked item whose `listing_id` is in that set before taking the top-N-by-margin slice, and notes how many were excluded. **Fails soft, not closed**: if the ad-campaign check itself errors, the section falls back to the unfiltered list with an explicit "(could not check ad status this run)" note, rather than silently dropping the whole section (a deliberate deviation from this codebase's usual fail-closed pattern, since this section is advisory/non-executing — showing a possibly-stale list beats showing nothing). **Verified live end-to-end**: real `--dry-run` against real production data correctly excluded exactly the 2 known ad-enrolled tracked items and correctly listed 5 others; `py_compile` clean.

      - **Real bug caught by a deliberate single-item live test before touching the automated batch**: the account's one campaign uses `adRateStrategy: DYNAMIC`. A first attempt sending an explicit `bidPercentage` (matching the rate already visible on existing ads, assumed to be the settable "basic rate") got a real `errorId 35010`: *"The bidPercentage should not be provided when selected adRateStrategy is DYNAMIC for the campaign."* Corrected: `_effective_bid_percentage()` now omits `bidPercentage` entirely for a DYNAMIC campaign (eBay manages the actual rate within the campaign's own configured cap — confirmed live the real assigned rates came back as 9.0%/10.0%, both under the account's 10% cap), and would only send an explicit flat rate for a non-DYNAMIC campaign type, which this account doesn't have. *(Full write-ups preserved for traceability, per this doc's own working contract — item numbers as originally assigned, not chronological.)*

47. **Item 47 — `branch11-dsers-mapping` timing out repeatedly starting 2026-09-13 13:56; root cause confirmed: DSers's stored refresh_token is dead again, needs Travis's one-time browser re-auth.** `DONE (2026-09-19, live end-to-end confirmed, Travis completed the required re-auth)`. **Real root cause, confirmed live 2026-09-13**: reproduced the hang directly (`uv run branch11_dsers_mapping.py`, output redirected to a file rather than piped through `head`, so a killed process's already-flushed writes wouldn't be lost) and got the real error: `Token refresh failed: 400` followed by the `=== DSers one-time authorization required ===` prompt with a fresh `mcp.dsers.com/oauth/authorize` URL — the exact same failure shape as Item 45's original `invalid_grant`. The refresh token is genuinely dead again; the script correctly falls into its one-time-reauth flow, but that flow needs a human to open the URL in a browser, which can never happen inside an unattended cron — so it sits waiting on `CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_S` (600s) until the cron's own 300s job timeout kills it first, every time.
    - **Self-correction, stated plainly**: my first pass at diagnosing this (same run, before the fix above) wrongly concluded the hang produced "zero output" and happened *before* `dsers_session()` was ever reached — that read came from piping a `timeout`-killed process's stdout through `head`, where the tool only surfaces output after the whole command exits and the already-flushed writes weren't visible in that capture path. Redirecting to a file instead (`> /tmp/item47_debug.log 2>&1`, read after the `timeout` command fully returned) showed the real sequence: the eligible-count print, every one of my temporary debug prints through `before session.initialize()`, then the real `Token refresh failed: 400` error — i.e. it was never a silent hang with no diagnostic output at all, it was a normal, already-instrumented-for-Item-45 auth failure whose printed prompt just wasn't being captured by my first test method. Worth remembering for future live debugging in this environment: prefer file redirection over piping through `head`/`tail` when a command might be killed by `timeout`.
    - **Ruled out (still valid from the prior pass)**: lock contention (`lsof` showed nothing holding `branch11_dsers_oauth.lock`) and a basic DSers-outage (`curl` to `mcp.dsers.com` responded fast). Both true, just not the actual cause — the real cause was the dead refresh token surfacing through the *already-existing* interactive-reauth path.
    - **Diagnostic instrumentation added and then fully reverted this run**: temporarily added `print(..., flush=True)` bracketing each `await`/`async with` in `dsers_session()`/`_dsers_session_locked()` in `branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py` to localize the hang; once the real error was captured, removed all of it and confirmed `py_compile` clean — the shipped file is unchanged from before this investigation.
    - **What's needed**: exactly Item 45's original resolution path — Travis completes the one-time browser OAuth flow (open the URL from a live run's real-time output, e.g. via `uv run branch11_dsers_mapping.py` run interactively) to mint a fresh refresh token. Not something this cron can do itself (Hard Constraint: DSers credentials need explicit per-use human action for anything beyond the already-established unattended refresh pattern, and a fresh authorization grant is exactly that). No code change needed unless Travis wants the *unattended* failure mode improved (e.g., wrapping `dsers_session()` in an outer `asyncio.wait_for()` well under 300s so a dead-refresh-token day fails fast with a clear alert instead of consuming the full cron timeout on every retry) — that would be a real, worthwhile follow-up engineering task once this immediate block clears, not urgent to build blind right now.
    - **Real scope of impact, confirmed same as before**: `branch11-dsers-mapping` itself is low-stakes, but `branch11-daily-item-research`/`branch11-price-check`/`branch11-enrichment` share the same `dsers_session()` and will hit the identical dead-refresh-token failure the next time any of them needs a real DSers call — worth watching their next real runs.
    - **Escalation confirmed live, 2026-09-14's 8am run**: `branch11-daily-item-research` hit its first-ever `error` today, real cron-recorded evidence — discovery completed normally (31 real candidates written to `branch11_ebay_only_2026-09-14.json`), but `branch11_dsers_search.py` then hit the exact same dead-refresh-token wall, this time running to its own full `CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_S=600` before raising a loud `TimeoutError` (this cron's own job timeout is long enough, ~1700s, that it wasn't killed mid-wait the way `dsers-mapping`'s tighter 300s timeout was). **Real, concrete business impact, not just a housekeeping gap**: `branch11_candidates_2026-09-14.json` was never written (confirmed missing) and `branch11_auto_listed.json`'s last real write is still 2026-09-13 13:26 (my own diagnostic test yesterday, not a real listing) — meaning today's daily candidate-judging, auto-listing, and email report to Travis never ran at all. This is the first day Item 47 has visibly broken more than `dsers-mapping`'s own minor SKU-mapping housekeeping; it now blocks the whole daily discovery→list→report pipeline until Travis completes the one-time re-auth.
    - **Third confirmed occurrence, same day, 13:00 `branch11-price-check`**: identical failure signature (`Token refresh failed: 400`, `durationMs: 603387` ≈ the same ~600s `CALLBACK_TIMEOUT_S` wait) after successfully reading the 23-entry ledger but before ever reaching DSers. Today's price-check/margin/advertising email also never sent to Travis. Noon's `branch11-enrichment` was the one exception so far, but only because it had zero eligible candidates this run (`0/4 slots filled`, ~1s) — it likely never opened a real DSers session, so it doesn't count as evidence the issue is patchy; every DSers-touching cron that actually needed to call DSers today has failed identically. Confirms Item 47 is a full, not partial, block on Branch 11's daily automation until Travis's one-time re-auth.
    - **Duration escalation, 2026-09-17 23:00**: `branch11_dsers_mcp_tokens.json` has now gone unwritten for 4 days 14+ hours straight (last real write 2026-09-13 07:56:33) — confirmed still unchanged as of this check. In that span: `branch11-daily-item-research` has failed 4 consecutive mornings (2026-09-14 through -17, one real discovery-only run each day with zero candidate-judging/auto-listing/email after), `branch11-price-check` has failed 4 consecutive afternoons (zero price-drift/advertising emails), and `branch11-dsers-mapping` has failed every 30-minute tick without exception (its own cron-reported error counter has hit the `openclaw cron list` display's `99+x` cap). This is a real, still-growing gap in Branch 11's daily automation, not a one-off — flagging the elapsed time plainly since the root cause (needs Travis's one-time browser re-auth, same as Item 45) hasn't changed and no code action is available to shorten it further.

No change in root cause or remedy — still needs Travis's one-time browser re-auth. Not re-deriving the full write-up each day; this is a brief pointer that the block is ongoing and growing, not new analysis.
    - **7th consecutive failed day confirmed live, 2026-09-19 08:00**: `branch11-daily-item-research` fired and failed again (`error (6x)`) — discovery still works fine (52 real candidates written to `branch11_ebay_only_2026-09-19.json`), but the DSers step hit the same dead-token wall, this time surfaced as `Token refresh failed: 503` (transient-flavored HTTP code, but same `=== DSers one-time authorization required ===` reauth prompt as every prior occurrence — not a new/different failure class, still the same dead refresh_token). Token file still unchanged since 2026-09-13 07:56:33 (6 days). No change in root cause or remedy.

Deliberately did not touch or use the token myself (no DSers calls made) — per the Hard Constraints, credential use stays with the pipeline's own scripts/crons, verification here was file-metadata/structure only.

Combined with `branch11-dsers-mapping`'s 09:17 success (2 SKUs mapped, logged above) and noon's `branch11-enrichment` running its normal incremental pattern, every DSers-touching cron has now had a real confirmed-working post-fix run except `branch11-daily-item-research` (next real chance is tomorrow's 8am). Every Branch 11 cron has now had at least one fully successful real run since the 2026-09-19 09:07 re-auth — Item 47's fix is confirmed holding across the entire pipeline, not just individually-tested pieces.
