46. **Item 46 — node5 (local LLM) as last-resort variant-aspect resolution when daily auto-listing falls short of Travis's item goal.** `DONE (2026-09-04, via chat, Travis-directed)`. Real, HIGH/MEDIUM-verdict, profit-passing candidates were failing purely on a variant-aspect decision (Color/Size/etc. with no generic eBay value and no deterministic textual evidence) — real production example the same day, `branch11-386432484811`. Travis's explicit decision: autonomous (not chat-gated), specifically because these items are already high-confidence matches — the only missing piece is one decision, and node5 (Ollama, `http://100.97.38.121:11434`, zero token cost) is well-suited to make it.
    - **Real, serious problem found via live testing before this went anywhere near production — self-reported model confidence is not trustworthy.** Two real held-out candidates tested against `llama3.1:8b`: (1) a dimension-blocked shelf where the model claimed "the dimensions text explicitly lists '31cm'" — the real captured `dimension_text` has no measurements at all; (2) a Color-blocked laptop stand where the model picked `Color: Silver` "with high confidence" reasoning "'Metal' implies a metallic color" — no color is ever stated in the real source text. Both are confident, plausible-sounding fabrication, exactly the failure mode this whole pipeline's "never guess" philosophy exists to prevent.
    - **Fix: grounding enforced in code, not just requested in the prompt.** Every "high confidence" answer must now include a `quote` field — a verbatim substring of the real source text. `_quote_appears_in_source()` (`branch11_llm_assist.py`) independently verifies the quote is genuinely a substring of the actual concatenated source text (case/whitespace-insensitive) before the answer is trusted at all; a fabricated or missing quote is rejected regardless of stated confidence. **Re-tested both original failure cases against both node5 models (`mistral-nemo:12b` and `llama3.1:8b`) after the fix — both correctly refused both cases post-fix.** Also verified the mechanism still succeeds on a case with genuine verbatim evidence present (both models correctly extracted `Color: Black` from "Matte Black Finish"), confirming this isn't just uniformly refusing everything.
    - **Confirmed the real AliExpress text-extraction step (`PRODUCT_PAGE_JS`/`_verify_ali_match()`, Item 26) is what feeds this** — it runs before the aspect-resolution block in `run_auto_listing()`'s pool loop, so `match["description"]`/`match["dimension_text"]` already hold the real, fully-captured page text (ld+json description + specifications + measurement-bearing lines) by the time node5 is asked — nothing missing or truncated on the way in.
    - **Model choice**: `mistral-nemo:12b` set as the default (node5's "reasoning" model per its own real config, matching Travis's framing of llama3.1:8b as the everyday/execution model) — `model=` is a one-line override. Both models performed identically across the 3 real/synthetic test cases run (no observed quality difference yet at this sample size).
    - **Wired into `run_auto_listing()`**: after Item 25 Phase 3's check finds no genuine multi-value opportunity (this is a distinct, narrower fallback — one confident single value per aspect, not a multi-SKU variation group), for a HIGH/MEDIUM-verdict, profit-passing candidate, `branch11_llm_assist.resolve_aspects_via_llm()` is tried before giving up. A full resolution (every unresolved aspect answered) is passed as `manual_aspects` into the existing, already-trusted `list_candidate()` path — no new eBay API surface. The trigger condition ("only when short of the daily goal") is satisfied automatically by the loop's own pre-existing `if listed_count >= top_n: break` guard — no separate check needed, and no extra node5 calls happen once the goal is already met.
    - **Real bug found and fixed during wiring**: the first draft of the success path duplicated a stripped-down copy of the normal ledger-write logic and silently dropped the WooCommerce mirror call plus several fields (`ali_url`/`ali_price`/`ali_id`/`woo_product_id`/`dsers_mapped`) that Step 8 (order monitoring) and the DSers-mapping cron both depend on. Refactored into one shared `_record_successful_listing()` helper, called from both the normal and LLM-assisted success paths, so they can't drift apart again.
    - **Audit trail**: every node5 call (prompt inputs, raw response, parsed result) logged to `branch11_llm_assist_log.json`. **Email report**: an LLM-assisted listing is shown in the normal "AUTO-LISTED THIS RUN" section (it's a real, normal listing) with an added distinct `LLM-ASSISTED LISTING -- node5 resolved: {...}` line, so it's never silently indistinguishable from a fully-deterministic listing even though the mechanism is autonomous.
    - **Scope, deliberately narrower than it could be**: only handles the `needs_unresolvable_variation` gate (variant-enabled aspects), matching Travis's own framing ("decisions on variant data"). A related but distinct real failure mode exists — `build_item_aspects()` failing inside `list_candidate()` on a *non-variation-enabled* required aspect (real example the same day, `branch11-386432484811`'s own error) — not wired to LLM-assist yet, flagged as a natural small follow-up, not done today to keep scope matched to what was actually asked.
    - **Confirmed live 2026-09-05, the first real cron run since this shipped — fired for real, worked correctly, both the resolve and refuse paths.** Three real candidates needed a `Type` decision (`branch11-165439481792`/`branch11-154939337833`/`branch11-154940366560`, all "Portable External HDD"-shaped titles); node5 (`mistral-nemo:12b`) resolved all three identically and correctly, grounded in a real verbatim quote (`'Portable 2.5 External Hard Drive'`), and 2 of the 3 were successfully auto-listed via LLM-assist. The 3rd's aspects were also correctly resolved but the actual `publish_offer` call failed downstream for an unrelated reason — a genuine eBay policy block (`errorId 25002`, "identical item already listed" against a different existing SKU) — not a node5/LLM-assist failure; the auto-list loop correctly moved on to the next pool candidate per Item 36's replenishment logic. Separately, a 4th real candidate (`branch11-335251351456`, a `Color` decision) got a real refusal — `"no high-confidence answer -- unresolved"` — confirming the grounding check's refuse path also works correctly live, not just in the pre-ship test cases. This is a clean, real confirmation of the whole mechanism working as designed on its first live exposure.

45. **Item 45 — DSers's stored refresh_token is dead (`invalid_grant`); ALL DSers-dependent automation was blocked.** `DONE (2026-09-04, real root cause found and fixed, Travis completed the required re-auth)`. Originally raised 2026-09-03 as a cron-driven production-readiness item; Item 44's own write-up already flagged the real gap ("a full fix would need a lock held across the whole check-then-refresh-then-write sequence, a bigger redesign not attempted here") — this closes exactly that gap.
    - **Real root cause, pinned down via timestamps, not assumed**: `branch11-dsers-mapping` (every ~30 min) ran clean, fast (~3s) refreshes from 15:48 through 19:48 on 2026-09-03. The first failure was at **20:18:49** — exactly the access token's 6-hour expiry boundary from its **14:18:51** issuance, and every attempt since failed identically (20+ in a row over 24h, `openclaw cron runs --id 01a07041-352c-4a9c-94b2-a33292c3e2ef`). That total, permanent failure pattern (not an occasional blip) pointed at the token itself being dead from the moment it was written, not a policy-driven periodic expiry. Checked the vendored `mcp` SDK directly (`oauth2.py`): a failed refresh's `clear_tokens()` only clears in-memory state, never touches disk — so a losing racer can't be corrupting the file on its own failure path. That leaves only one mechanism: **two independent processes refreshed concurrently and only one's rotated token survived being written to disk, while DSers itself no longer honors it.** Confirmed the opportunity for exactly that: the 14:18:51 token was minted by an off-schedule manual catch-up run of `branch11_daily_run.sh`, and `branch11-dsers-mapping`'s own regular tick fired at **14:18:49** — two seconds earlier, on its own unrelated schedule. Also confirmed there was no cross-job locking to have prevented this: `branch11_daily_run.sh`, `branch11_dsers_mapping_run.sh`, `branch11_price_check_run.sh`, and `branch11_enrichment`'s wrapper each only had their **own** per-job `flock` (`/tmp/branch11-<job>.lock`), preventing a job from overlapping *with itself*, never with a *different* job — and `FileTokenStorage`'s existing lock (Item 44's fix) only covers the brief disk read/write, not the "check expiry → call DSers → write result" round trip where the actual race lives.
    - **Real fix**: `branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py`'s `dsers_session()` now acquires a new dedicated cross-process lock (`OAUTH_LOCK_FILE`, a plain `flock` held for the session's *entire* lifetime, not just the refresh) before any of the four independent DSers-touching cron jobs (`branch11-daily-item-research`, `branch11-dsers-mapping`, `branch11-price-check`, `branch11-enrichment`) can touch DSers auth at all — fully serializes them, closing the race regardless of which scripts happen to fire together in the future. Released automatically even on a crash/SIGKILL (an OS-level `flock` tied to the process's own fd, not a lock-file-exists convention that could go stale). **Verified the mechanism directly**: a real two-process test (`multiprocessing`, one holds the lock 2s, the other tries to acquire immediately) confirmed the second process only acquires after the first releases — real serialization, not just code that looks right. **Verified live in production, not just in isolation**: while testing the re-auth flow, a real `branch11-dsers-mapping` cron tick fired mid-test and was observed correctly blocking (`lsof` showed it holding `6w` — waiting — against the lock file) instead of racing.
    - **What only Travis could do — completed 2026-09-04**: the dead refresh_token itself needed a real human completing a real browser consent; no code fix regenerates that. Took several attempts to actually land purely due to environment mechanics, not the OAuth flow itself: (1) the browser was opened before a listener existed to catch the callback; (2) a live `branch11-dsers-mapping` cron tick was mid-flight holding the new session lock; (3) most of the real delay was an `ssh -L 8734:localhost:8734` tunnel being run from a shell already SSH'd into node1 instead of from Travis's own laptop, so the forward looped back on node1 and never reached his laptop's browser — corrected once identified. **Confirmed genuinely fixed, not just "no error"**: a live auth check against the real DSers MCP server returned `AUTH OK, tools: ['dsers_store_discover', 'dsers_rules_validate', 'dsers_product_import', 'dsers_product_preview', 'dsers_product_update_rules']`, and `branch11_dsers_mcp_tokens.json` shows a fresh write (first one since 2026-09-03 14:18:51).
    - **Real-world impact**: the 2026-09-04 8am `branch11-daily-item-research` run produced zero new listings (discovery alone doesn't list anything) — recovered via a same-day catch-up run of `branch11_daily_run.sh` once auth was restored (see this doc's Run Log for the outcome).
    - **Follow-up, same day (Travis-directed): added direct alerting, since prevention isn't the same as detection.** The lock above closes this specific incident's cause, but nothing stops DSers from killing the token for an unrelated reason someday (manual revocation, a policy expiry, an incident on their side) — and when that happens, the only signal today is silent cron failures piling up in `openclaw cron list` and repeated "still blocked" lines in this doc's Run Log, which is exactly why this incident sat broken a full day before Travis noticed listings had stopped. **Built**: `_send_auth_alert_email()` in `branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py`, called from `_redirect_handler()` (fires the moment any of the four DSers-touching scripts falls through to the interactive-auth-required path) — reuses the same Gmail App Password `branch11_email_report.py` already uses (`branch11_unattended_creds.json`), emails the real authorize URL plus the exact laptop-vs-node1-terminal instructions that caused most of today's real delay. **Deduped correctly**: keyed off the token file's own `tokens_issued_at` via a new marker file (`branch11_dsers_auth_alert_state.json`) — all four jobs hitting the same dead-token incident (even across many hours, e.g. `branch11-dsers-mapping`'s every-30-min retries) send exactly one email, not one per attempt, and a future *different* incident (a new `tokens_issued_at` after the next real fix) correctly triggers a fresh alert. No new concurrency risk: `OAUTH_LOCK_FILE` already fully serializes all four jobs' sessions, so only one process can ever be in this alert path at a time. **Verified**: real dedup test (same generation → no send, different generation → sends, marker updates correctly) with `smtplib.SMTP` mocked so no real email went out during testing; `py_compile` clean; confirmed the real credentials file already has the right shape (`gmail.address`/`gmail.app_password`) so this needs no new setup.

40. **Item 40 — the AUTO-LISTED section of the daily email didn't show the fee/AliExpress-price breakdown, making the profit math unverifiable and looking wrong.** `DONE (2026-09-01, via chat, Travis flagged live)`. Travis's report: the email's total profit "seems to be just eBay price minus AliExpress matched item price" (no fee subtracted). **Checked the actual math first**: `compute_profit()` (`branch11_pipeline.py`) was already correctly computing `total_profit = ebay_price - ebay_fee - ali_price - ali_shipping` — verified against real ledger data ($20.99 - $3.57 fee - $10.17 - $1.99 = $5.26, matches exactly). **The real bug**: `_auto_listed_block()` in `branch11_email_report.py` only ever printed `"eBay price $X, total profit $Y"` — no fee or AliExpress price shown anywhere in that section, so the correct math was unverifiable from the email itself, which is exactly why it looked wrong. `_passed_block()` already showed the full breakdown via the shared `_profit_line()` helper; `_auto_listed_block()` just needed the same treatment. **Fix**: now calls `_profit_line()` too. **Verified live** with a real dry-run against today's actual candidates file (2026-09-01): shows `"eBay price $54.99"` then `"Profit breakdown: fee $9.35, target margin $8.80, budget $36.84, ali price $2.92, ali shipping $1.99, headroom $31.93, TOTAL PROFIT $40.73"` — fully transparent, and the arithmetic checks out (54.99 − 9.35 − 2.92 − 1.99 = 40.73). Takes effect starting tomorrow's email; today's already-sent email wasn't resent since the underlying number was never actually wrong, just not shown.
    - **Bonus finding while testing**: today's 8am cron ran fully unattended for the first time since yesterday's fixes (OAuth, replenishment, exclusion list, aspect resolution) — cron status is now `ok` (was `error`), 25 items scanned (healthy range), 1 real item auto-listed, 1 correctly declined for genuinely needing Variations API support (Item 25 phase 3, working as designed, not a bug).

20. **Item 20 — verify `suggest_category()`'s Taxonomy API accuracy at real volume.** `DONE (2026-09-01, cron-driven production-readiness run)` — verification task, factual answer delivered, no code changes (none were warranted by the evidence found). **Real method**: pulled every real `category_analysis` record (`category_id`/`category_name`/`match_score`) across every available `branch11_candidates_*.json` (2026-08-25 through 2026-09-01) — 204 rows, 188 unique real eBay-item titles, a genuine multi-day production sample, not a synthetic test set. Spot-checked the full range: the 20 lowest-`match_score` items, the 15 around the median, and the top 15.
    - **Real accuracy is high: 3 genuine miscategorizations found out of 188 (≈1.6%)**, and both patterns are narrow and identifiable, not a systemic taxonomy problem: (1) **two macrame candidates whose titles contain explicit vintage-year language** — `"Vintage 1982 Mac Pac Angel Fish Macrame Kit..."` and `"Artcraft Concepts No. 1381 Reindeer Wall Hanging Macram[e]..."` — got pulled to a `"1970s"` decade/collectibles category instead of eBay's own genuine, correct `"Macramé Kits"`/`"Wall Hangings"` leaf categories (which 25 of the other 27 real macrame candidates in the same sample correctly resolved to, several scoring 89-97). The likely cause: eBay's Taxonomy API's title-based search reading "vintage"/an explicit decade-adjacent year as a genuine collectibles signal, not unreasonable given the literal text, even though these are current-manufacture items using nostalgic branding. (2) **one laptop stand** — `"Metal Laptop Stand with Tray Design for Study Table Use Daily"` → `"Home Office Desks"` (wrong; every other real laptop-stand candidate in the sample correctly resolved to `"Stands, Holders & Car Mounts"`).
    - **Checked whether any of the 3 real misses ever reached production**: none did — cross-referenced all 3 titles against `branch11_auto_listed.json`, zero matches. No real-world listing has ever shipped with a wrong category; the miscategorization rate is real but has caused zero actual harm to date, since none of these 3 were ever profitable/high enough to clear the daily auto-list cutoff.
    - **A second, distinct finding: `match_score` (the self-computed 0-100 confidence signal `analyze_category()` returns) is a poor predictor of real accuracy at the low end**, though a reliable one at mid/high scores. Of the 20 lowest-scoring real candidates (scores 22-45), roughly half were genuinely *correct* categorizations that simply scored low — most visibly 5 different supplement/vitamin titles (`"Hylauronic Acid 850mg 30 Tablets"`, `"2 PACK Resilia Oil of Oregano..."`, etc.) all correctly resolving to `"Vitamins & Minerals"` at scores as low as 33. Root cause matches the exact failure mode `analyze_category()`'s own docstring already documents from its original design iteration (the "United States, Country Flags" case): long, marketing-heavy titles dilute the per-word fuzzy-match average against a short category name, even when the category itself is completely correct. **Confirmed this has no real behavioral consequence**: grepped every use of `match_score` in the codebase — it's read in exactly one place, `branch11_email_report.py`'s informational display line (`"eBay category: 'X' (match Y)"`), never used to gate `needs_unresolvable_variation`, auto-list eligibility, or any other real decision. So the miscalibration only makes Travis's email look slightly less confident than warranted on some correct categorizations — not a source of any real listing error.
    - **Also checked, since it directly bears on category accuracy's practical impact: Item 26's already-documented "category-suggestion granularity mismatch" finding** (a correct broad category, e.g. "Tables," whose offered dimension-aspect range is calibrated for larger items than the specific one sourced) is a real, distinct, already-folded-in pattern per that item's own write-up — reconfirmed in scope for this item, not new.
    - **Ruled out one apparent false alarm found while sampling**: several mouth-tape/anti-snoring titles still appeared in the historical sample with a category attached. Checked file provenance directly — every single one is from `branch11_candidates_2026-08-31.json` (the day *before* Item 39's discovery-time exclusion list shipped); zero appear in the 2026-09-01 file. Confirms Item 39's exclusion filter has no live gap — this was stale pre-filter data, not a current issue.
    - **No fix implemented**: given the real miscategorization rate is low (≈1.6%), has caused zero real-world harm so far, and the one identifiable recurring pattern (vintage-year language → decade/collectibles category) has exactly 2 known real instances — thin evidence for a curated fix, per this codebase's own established principle (see Item 19's deliberately-not-built "core-noun check", held off on zero evidence; here the evidence is real but still very sparse). Worth revisiting with a targeted fix (e.g. a curated check that a "vintage"/explicit-year title shouldn't resolve to a decade/collectibles-path category unless corroborated elsewhere) only if this pattern recurs and grows, not preemptively.

22. **Item 22 — daily DSers-based price-check email.** `DONE (2026-09-01/09-02, via chat, Travis-directed, expanded scope)`. Original design was a weekly AliExpress-scrape check; Travis expanded it: **DSers cost as the price source** (what's actually used for fulfillment, not a fresh AliExpress scrape), **daily at 1 PM Central** (not weekly), plus two email features — flag price drift bad enough to need a manual eBay price update, and a placeholder section flagging high-margin items as future advertising candidates. Planned via `EnterPlanMode` given the real architectural decisions involved (data model, flag sensitivity, schedule) — plan approved before building.
    - **Confirmed live**: `dsers_my_products(store_id, keyword=<ebay_title>)` already returns a real `cost` field for any DSers-mapped product — no new DSers capability needed, reuses the exact same title-based lookup + exact-match safety check `branch11_dsers_mapping.py` (Item 37) already validated.
    - **Confirmed with Travis**: price-drift flagging fires only when the new cost would make the item fail its *original* target margin (not on every minor cent-level move) — filters currency/rounding noise, surfaces only what needs real attention.
    - **New**: `branch11_price_check.py` (standalone DSers session, zero LLM), `branch11_price_check.json` (new ledger — a per-SKU `baseline_cost` established on first check and kept fixed, since `branch11_auto_listed.json`'s own `ali_price` field turned out to be unreliable — see next bullet), `branch11_price_check_run.sh`, new `suggested_ebay_price()` helper in `branch11_pipeline.py` (the minimum eBay price that restores the original target margin at the new cost — Travis decides whether to actually apply it; Hard Constraints still forbid auto-editing a live listing's price). Reuses `compute_profit()` and `branch11_email_report.py`'s `send_email()`/`_money()` rather than duplicating them.
    - **Real bug found and fixed along the way**: `run_auto_listing()`'s ledger write stored `match.get("price")`, but `AliMatch` has no `price` field (only `prices`, a list) — `ali_price` was silently `None` on real ledger entries since the field was added 2026-08-28. Fixed to parse `prices` correctly (same pattern `_build_pipeline_result()` already uses). Doesn't affect this new feature (which keeps its own baseline), but worth closing out since it's directly adjacent.
    - **Real floating-point edge case found and fixed**: `suggested_ebay_price()`'s exact break-even price, at standard rounding, could land one cent short of the target margin (`compute_profit()` on the suggested price itself reported `passes: False`). Fixed by rounding UP to the next cent instead of nearest — verified the suggested price now always actually clears the margin.
    - **Real data-quality issue found live**: DSers itself reports `cost: {min: 0, max: 0}` for one real mapped product (a Makita power tool) — genuinely DSers-side data, not an extraction bug, and obviously not a real $0 cost. Added a guard treating cost `<= 0` the same as missing data (`NO_COST_DATA` status) rather than trusting it — prevents a false ~100%-margin item from polluting the advertising-candidates placeholder ranking.
    - **Verified**: an isolated unit test of the flag condition + `suggested_ebay_price()` (a case that stays passing, a case that flips to failing, confirmed the suggested price genuinely clears the margin). Then a full live run against all 11 real DSers-mapped ledger entries — 10 checked cleanly (baselines established), 1 correctly routed to `NO_COST_DATA`. Ran twice to confirm baselines persist (no `[NEW BASELINE]` tag on the second run) while `last_checked_cost`/`last_checked_at` still update. Ran the real wrapper script for real, including actually sending the email — confirmed working end-to-end (subject, all three sections, SMTP credentials).
    - **Cron registered and verified (2026-09-01, via chat, Travis ran the commands)** — see Item 28's write-up below for the full multi-cron verification pass (two real registration bugs caught and fixed live: `branch11-enrichment`'s dropped flags from a broken shell line-continuation, and this item's own cron initially registered with `--timeout-seconds 90` instead of `900`, fixed via `cron edit df3804e0-f013-4ead-8afd-d70bd917da8f --timeout-seconds 900`).
    - **Advertising-candidates section is deliberately unfinished** — a plain top-5-by-current-margin list with a "not yet actionable, mechanism TBD" note, exactly as Travis asked. No real selection/advertising-integration logic exists yet; that's a separate future item (Item 41) once Travis decides what "investigate options" should look like.

26. **Item 26 — pull real item dimensions (L/H/W) from the AliExpress source.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed) — mechanism confirmed correct and working; real-world yield on the tested sample is honestly low, root cause fully traced, not a code gap`. Built (`PRODUCT_PAGE_JS` in `branch11_pipeline.py` captures the Specifications table + dimension-bearing body text; `_extract_dimension_inches()`/`_resolve_aspect_value()`'s dimension tier in `branch11_ebay_listing.py` parses it, converts units, snaps to eBay's nearest offered "N in" bucket, never force-fitting outside the real offered range). Three real bugs found and fixed during testing: (1) decimal points in measurements ("26.5cm") were corrupted by the clause-splitting regex; (2) body-text scraping was picking up unrelated cross-sell/"recommended for you" carousel text nested inside the same container (fixed by hiding `[class*="fusion-card"], [class*="fusion-page-card"]` elements before reading text); (3) added `"base to top"` as a recognized Item Height context phrase after finding it's a common real AliExpress spec-table key ("Base to top distance").
    - **Final live re-verification completed against the full 29-candidate historical set: 0/29 fully resolved.** Traced the "base to top" fix specifically (it was expected to help) to understand why: for "Folding Tray Metal Side Table" (spec-table "Base to top distance: 44cm"), the extraction now correctly parses 44cm → 17.32in → rounds to "17 in" — but eBay's `Item Height` values for that item's suggested category ("Tables", id 38204) only offer **28-43 in**, calibrated for regular/larger furniture. 17in is genuinely below the offered range, so the code *correctly* declines rather than force-fitting a wrong value — this is the extraction mechanism working exactly as designed, not a bug.
    - **Real conclusion**: two separate, distinct limiting factors, neither of which is a dimension-extraction problem: (a) most of these 29 candidates simply have no dimension data anywhere in scrapable page text at all (the dominant pattern — no parsing cleverness fixes missing data); (b) for the minority that do have real extractable measurements, the *suggested category's* offered dimension range often doesn't cover the item's actual (often smaller) real-world size — a category-suggestion granularity mismatch, the same class of problem Item 20 already investigated (see above — reconfirmed, not new).
    - **Marked done because the mechanism itself is correct, safe, and verified** (confirmed by direct trace, not just aggregate pass/fail) — it will resolve real cases going forward whenever both conditions are met (real dimension text present AND the item's real size falls inside its category's offered range), it just won't help most of *this specific* 29-item sample, honestly.

27. **Item 27 — batch discovery/matching with early-stop** once the daily auto-list target is reached. `DONE (2026-09-01, via chat, Travis-directed) — deliberately re-scoped smaller than the original proposal`. **Re-scoped after checking real current volume**: the original 2026-08-30 proposal envisioned a single interleaved discover+search+judge loop, sized for the era when runs found hundreds of items/day. Checked today's real number first: 25 items on 2026-09-01, a representative real day post-Item-39's exclusion list and the tile cap — the original problem is already substantially smaller than when this was proposed. **Implemented a smaller, lower-risk version**: `branch11_dsers_search.py` now judges each item inline (reusing `_build_pipeline_result()`/`build_dsers_ali_match()` from `branch11_pipeline.py` — the *exact* same functions `--stage finish`'s Pass 1 already uses, so early-stop's eligibility check can never drift from what Pass 1 would decide later) and stops calling `dsers_find_product` once enough HIGH-verdict, profit-passing candidates are found to fill the replenishment pool (`auto_list_top_n + replenish_buffer`, default 10 — correctly targets the Item 36 pool size, not just `auto_list_top_n`). Discovery (Step 1) stays a separate, untouched, already-cheap, already-capped full pass — not folded into the loop, a deliberate scope-narrowing from the original "single driver" vision. `--stage finish` needs zero changes: it just receives a smaller `dsers_search_<date>.json` file on an early-stop day. New `--no-early-stop` flag to disable entirely if ever needed, plus `--early-stop-pool-size`/`--fee-pct`/`--margin-pct` overrides.
    - **Verified live against real 2026-09-01 data** (using scratch output paths): (a) default pool size (10) against the real 25-item file correctly did NOT early-stop, since only 2 eligible candidates existed that day — processed all 25, exactly matching old behavior when there's nothing to skip; (b) a deliberately low test threshold (`--early-stop-pool-size 2`) correctly stopped at item 7/25 once 2 eligible candidates were found, skipping the remaining 18; (c) confirmed `--stage finish` consumes an early-stopped (smaller) file with no errors.
    - **Real incident during this verification, disclosed in full**: `branch11_pipeline.py --stage finish` has no output-path override — it always writes to `branch11_candidates_{today's real date}.json`/`_latest.json` regardless of which `--dsers-search-file` is passed in. A verification run against test data **overwrote today's real 25-item candidates file** with a 7-item early-stop-test result. **No real-world state was affected** — the actual eBay listing, WooCommerce mirror, and ledger (`branch11_auto_listed.json`) were all confirmed intact throughout, since none of those live in the candidates JSON. **Fully recovered**: re-ran a full, non-early-stopped DSers search for real (25/25 items) into a scratch path, rebuilt the candidates file via `--stage finish --no-auto-list` (critical — avoided triggering any new real listing attempts during recovery), then patched the one row with real-world consequences (the actually-listed SKU) to match the ledger's authoritative `ali_url` and the exact original profit breakdown (captured earlier in the same chat session, before the mistake) — verified byte-for-byte identical to the original email output afterward. The other 24 (never-listed, informational-only) rows reflect a fresh independent DSers re-search rather than the exact original snapshot, since DSers match results can drift between calls and no backup of the exact original judged data existed — low-consequence, disclosed rather than hidden. **Lesson for future testing**: never invoke `--stage finish` against a day that already has a real candidates file without either (a) accepting it will overwrite today's real file, or (b) testing only against a past/synthetic date. There is no scratch-output-path option for this command today — worth a small follow-up fix (a `--date`/`--out-file` override) if `--stage finish` needs testing against real DSers data again.

28. **Item 28 — daily description/variant-data enrichment process** (tracked, small-batch). `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed)`, folded together with Item 31 below since both turned out to be the same build (OCR *is* the enrichment content). New `branch11_enrichment.py`: walks `branch11_auto_listed.json` oldest-listed-first, 4 items/run, skips anything already in the new `branch11_enrichment.json` ledger or missing an `ali_url` — the full listed-item history gets covered incrementally over many days, never reprocessed. Non-LLM (`branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py`'s standalone DSers session, same as steps 2/5). Runs via new `branch11_enrichment_run.sh`, its own dedicated ~12 PM cron:
    ```bash
    openclaw cron add "branch11-enrichment" \
      --cron "0 12 * * *" \
      --command "flock -n /tmp/branch11-enrichment.lock /home/travis/openclaw-troubleshooting/branch11_enrichment_run.sh" \

      --timeout-seconds 900

A DEWALT battery's images OCR'd to real spec content ("BMS SMART PROTECTION... Safe for Heavy Duty Work..."); a RYOBI battery's images yielded safety/spec text ("Temperature Protection... Overcurrent Protection..."); a makeup mirror's images yielded real usage instructions ("keep the mirror at a distance of 3-5 inches from the eyes"). Also captured real variant `options` data for genuinely multi-variant products (17-21 real color/pack-size combinations for the two batteries) — stored for Item 25 phase 3's future use, not acted on by this script.
    - **Never touches the live eBay or WooCommerce listing** — Hard Constraints forbid editing/pricing a real listing "after the fact" autonomously; this only writes to `branch11_enrichment.json` for Travis's own reference or future automated use once that's explicitly approved.
    - **Resolved (2026-08-31, Travis's decision): DSers import-list drafts are cleaned up automatically.** `branch11_enrichment.py` now calls `dsers_product_delete` right after each item's data is safely persisted (best-effort — a delete failure is recorded on the ledger entry as `cleanup_status` rather than treated as that item's failure). Verified live twice: (a) a fresh run's 4 items were confirmed genuinely absent from `dsers_import_list` afterward; (b) the 8 items from the two earlier enrichment runs (before this cleanup existed) were retroactively cleaned up the same way — all 8 confirmed `DELETED`.
    - **All 4 Branch 11 crons registered and verified (2026-09-01, via chat, Travis ran the commands)**. Two real registration bugs found and fixed live via `openclaw cron edit`, not just rubber-stamped: (1) this item's own `branch11-enrichment` — a terminal line-wrap broke the `cron add` command's backslash continuation mid-flag-list (visible as a literal `-bash: --command-cwd: command not found` in Travis's paste), so only `name`/`--cron`/`--command` actually reached the create call — `cwd`, `description`, `--no-deliver`, and `--timeout-seconds` were silently dropped, leaving it with no working directory, no description, `delivery.mode: "announce"`, and no explicit timeout (would have run under the CLI's bare 30s default, far too short for real OCR/image work). Fixed via `cron edit 52774b70-7691-4b22-879b-e06ed9262948 --command-cwd ... --description ... --no-deliver --timeout-seconds 900`, re-verified via `cron get`. (2) `branch11-price-check` (Item 22) — registered with `--timeout-seconds 90` instead of the intended `900` (15x too short for a real DSers session across ~10 items plus sending the email). Fixed via `cron edit df3804e0-f013-4ead-8afd-d70bd917da8f --timeout-seconds 900`, re-verified. `branch11-dsers-mapping` was correct as registered, no changes needed. `branch11-production-readiness` was already fixed in a prior chat turn (see item 0 above) — confirmed still correct (Travis's later paste of it was stale terminal scrollback from the original run, not a fresh re-run, resolved by checking `updatedAtMs` against `cron get`). All 4 confirmed live via `cron get`: correct schedule, command, cwd, description, `delivery.mode: "none"`, and timeout.

31. **Item 31 — OCR extraction of AliExpress description-image text.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed)` — see Item 28 above, same build. **Real pivot from the original plan**: the original design assumed scraping AliExpress's own page for description images, but that path was blocked (the real content lives in an iframe that consistently returns empty under automation, root cause never found, 2026-08-30). Researched live and found a better path instead: DSers's `dsers_product_import` already returns a clean `image_urls` array directly (no iframe, no rendering race condition) — and doesn't need an LLM session either, since `branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py` (built 2026-08-30, after this item's original notes were written) already gives standalone DSers MCP access. 32. **Item 32 — eBay `publish_offer` error 25604** ("Availability not found") on one 2026-08-30 auto-list attempt. `DONE (2026-09-01, via chat, Travis-directed)`. **Checked the Item 35 precedent first** (a systemic block disguised as a one-off) — but this one really is a one-off: checked every `branch11_candidates_*.json` since 2026-08-30, **zero recurrences** across dozens of real listing attempts over 3+ days. Full error text (the doc only had the truncated summary before): `"Input error. Seller Inventory Service can not publish the data. Availability not found. Please try again or contact customer support.."` — the item was "OTTlite Wireless Charging LED Makeup Mirror Light Magnifying" (`branch11-188282514038`).
    - **Root cause, evidence-based**: `list_candidate()`'s `create_inventory_item()` → `create_offer()` → `publish_offer()` are called back-to-back with zero delay between them. eBay's own error text explicitly says "please try again," pointing to a rare eventual-consistency lag on eBay's backend — the freshly-created inventory item's availability data not yet fully propagated when `publish_offer` queries it moments later. Not a systemic block; a transient backend race.
    - **Fix**: `list_candidate()` now retries `publish_offer` once, after a 5-second delay, specifically when it fails with `errorId 25604` — any other error still fails immediately as before, no change to that behavior. **Verified** with an isolated logic test: confirmed the retry fires and succeeds when the first attempt returns 25604, and confirmed it does *not* fire for a different error code (no unintended broadening).
    - Distinct from Item 35 (`errorId 25019`, Medical Devices policy) — confirmed these are genuinely different error codes/causes, not the same issue reappearing.

18. **Item 18 — "Drop Ship 10 Copy" question.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis confirmed)`. Accidental duplicate, never intentionally used — Travis deleted it from the eBay production account. Re-verified live: `select_shipping_policy()` now resolves the $9.99 tier unambiguously to `Drop Ship 10` (id `243228513013`), no more tie. Docstring in `branch11_pipeline.py` and `branch11-listing-rules.md` items 18/Step 6 updated to match.

19. **Item 19 — improve `judge_match()`'s heuristic.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed — lightweight/non-LLM path chosen, no LLM call needed)`. Researched with Travis in chat first: presented a tiered options menu (lightweight/non-LLM options first, an LLM-judgment call held in reserve) — Travis chose to implement the two Tier 1 items and test live starting with tomorrow's run.
    - **Implemented: construction-material mismatch check.** New `_MATERIAL_CLUSTERS` (metal/steel/aluminum/iron/alloy grouped as one cluster — casually synonymous in real listings — vs. separate wood/plastic/leather/fabric/glass/rattan/ceramic clusters) + `_cluster_hits()` helper, wired into `judge_match()` as a new hard-mismatch check (-35, hard cap), same pattern as the existing accessory/light/wired-wireless/brand checks. **Evidence-based**: the item's original real incident (2026-08-25, metal-frame dog crate matched to an Oxford-fabric item) plus a live scan of 7 days of historical judged output. **Verified live**: a simulated version of the original incident now correctly drops from a passing verdict to `NONE` (score 6) with an explicit "construction material mismatch (ebay: metal vs ali: fabric/oxford)" reason. **Honest calibration note**: the initial evidence-scan found "8 cases" using naive exact-word matching (no clustering) — after building the properly-clustered check, only 1 of those 8 turns out to be a genuine cross-material-family mismatch (an aluminum vs. plastic laptop holder, now correctly caught); the other 7 were steel/aluminum/iron/metal variance within the same practical material family, which the clustered design correctly leaves alone rather than flagging as false positives — good calibration, not a shortfall of the fix.
    - **Investigated and deliberately NOT implemented: "core product-type noun" check** (e.g. crate/cage/kennel vs. playpen/pen/fence/enclosure). `branch11-listing-rules.md`'s rubric table already *described* this as a signal, but it was never actually coded — a real gap between documented design and implementation. However, a live scan of the same 7 days of historical judged output for real pet-containment core-noun mismatches turned up **zero cases** — unlike the material check, there's no real evidence behind it. Per this rubric's own stated principle (fixes built from real caught examples, not a general classifier), held off rather than build a curated list with no proven benefit. `branch11-listing-rules.md` documents this decision; revisit only if a real example surfaces.
    - **Not touched**: Tier 2 ideas from the options discussion (extending the quantity/count-unit regex, stripping filler words from the overlap calculation) — real evidence exists for the quantity-regex idea too (10 cases found live, including non-excluded ones like mismatched supplement pack sizes), but Travis scoped this pass to Tier 1 only. Worth a future pass.
    - **Next**: no further live testing done in this pass — Travis's explicit call was to let it run for real starting with tomorrow's 8am cron rather than force additional live verification now. Watch the next few days' `branch11_candidates_<date>.json` for `construction material mismatch` reasons in the output to confirm it's firing correctly (and not over-firing) at real volume.

34. **Item 34 — DSers MCP OAuth token refresh bug broke the 2026-08-31 8am cron.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis approved fix + catch-up)`. Root cause was two stacked gaps in `branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py`'s use of the MCP SDK's `OAuthClientProvider`: (1) `FileTokenStorage` never recorded token issuance time, so the SDK's `_initialize()` (which loads stored tokens but never calls `update_token_expiry()`) treated a file-loaded token as valid forever, skipping preemptive refresh and only discovering expiry via a real 401 — which then triggers a *full interactive* OAuth flow (browser + localhost:8734), impossible under a headless cron; (2) once preemptive refresh was fixed and correctly attempted, it 404'd — `_get_token_endpoint()` falls back to a guessed `{base_url}/token` when `context.oauth_metadata` hasn't been discovered yet, and DSers's real token endpoint is `https://mcp.dsers.com/oauth/token`, not `https://mcp.dsers.com/token` (confirmed via DSers's own `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`).
    - **Fix applied:** `FileTokenStorage` now persists a `tokens_issued_at` epoch and returns a corrected (real-elapsed-time-adjusted) `expires_in` from `get_tokens()`. A new `_RefreshAwareOAuthClientProvider` subclass overrides `_initialize()` to (a) re-register the corrected expiry via `context.update_token_expiry()` and (b) eagerly fetch+install real OAuth metadata from DSers's well-known endpoint, so the token endpoint resolves correctly regardless of refresh-vs-interactive path. `dsers_session()` now uses this subclass. The existing (still-alive, DSers's 30-day auto-rotating) refresh token was confirmed live to work once both gaps were fixed — no human re-auth was needed after all.
    - **Verified:** standalone auth-only test (list_tools via `dsers_session()`) succeeded cleanly post-fix, no interactive callback triggered. Then ran today's actual catch-up using the fix: step 2 (`branch11_dsers_search.py`) on the 59 already-discovered items — 56/59 matched; step 3 (`--stage finish`) — 1 of the top-5 shortlist auto-listed (branch11-375196030649, listingId 800591689625), 2 excluded by the pre-existing "can't confidently resolve item aspect" safety rule (working as intended), 2 hit a new eBay `publish_offer` error (flagged as Item 35); step 4 sent the daily report email; step 5 (DSers mapping) ran cleanly (0 newly mapped — all 9 eligible ledger entries were still pending Travis's manual WooCommerce-import click at the time, expected/normal).
    - Tomorrow's 8am cron succeeded unattended with no code changes needed beyond what was shipped here.

35. **Item 35 — eBay `errorId 25019` is a category-wide Medical Devices policy block on anti-snoring/mouth-tape items, not a per-item content-filter quirk.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis decided)` — Travis's decision: exclude the category via a new discovery-time exclusion list, implemented as Item 39. **Full error body pulled live 2026-08-31** (the original discovery only had the truncated summary): `"This item is not permitted under the Medical Devices policy... Please do not relist."`, linking to `https://www.ebay.com/help/policies/prohibited-restricted-items/medicine-medical-devices-healthcare-products-policy?id=4322`. Confirmed **4-for-4** on every mouth-tape/anti-snoring item attempted that day (NIB Mouth Tape 90 Strips, sNOre 90-day, REM Tape XL, REM Tape 30 Strips, the last three re-attempted after Item 38's aspect-resolution fix successfully resolved their `Type` aspect — aspects were fine, `publish_offer` itself was blocked). This is eBay policy, not a bug — nothing in this codebase can fix it. Per eBay's own "please do not relist" instruction, the pipeline did not attempt the remaining `NOT_LISTED` mouth-tape items that day.
    - **Resolved**: Travis chose a keyword-based exclusion list at discovery time (earliest possible point, not just a listing-time block) — see Item 39. Scope for now is just this one category (`mouth tape`/`anti-snoring`/`snore`/`snoring`); the broader "Health & Beauty generally" question wasn't raised and isn't assumed — extend `EXCLUDED_CATEGORIES` if another eBay-policy-blocked category turns up the same way this one did, don't preemptively widen it.
    - **Residual state**: 3 unpublished draft offers/inventory items were created on the production eBay account (offer IDs `251122378011`, `251122522011`, `251122698011`) during the 2026-08-31 re-attempts — never published, not visible to buyers, left as-is (no cleanup routine exists for failed listing attempts anywhere in this codebase).
    - Distinct from Item 32 (`errorId 25604`, "Availability not found") — don't conflate the two.

36. **Item 36 — replenish the auto-list shortlist on failure, instead of leaving slots unfilled.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed)`. Travis flagged this directly after reviewing that day's run — 4 of the original top-5 shortlist failed that day, leaving 7 HIGH-passing candidates sitting `NOT_LISTED` with zero attempt even though they met every criterion.
    - **Implemented in `branch11_pipeline.py`**: the old design (Pass 2 in `main()`'s `--stage finish` eagerly pre-verified exactly `auto_list_top_n` candidates, then `run_auto_listing()` attempted only those, no retry on failure) is replaced by lazy, bounded replenishment. New `AUTO_LIST_REPLENISH_BUFFER_DEFAULT = 5` constant and `--auto-list-replenish-buffer` CLI flag. New `select_auto_list_pool(out, pool_size, already_listed)` builds a profit-sorted pool of size `auto_list_top_n + replenish_buffer` (default 10) — same HIGH-verdict/profit-passing/not-already-listed/dedup-by-ali_id filter as `select_auto_list_candidates()`, but without requiring `description`/`category_analysis` already present, since most of the pool isn't verified yet. `run_auto_listing()` now walks that pool one candidate at a time: verifies (`_verify_ali_match()`, one browser page visit) and recomputes `category_analysis` lazily, only for a candidate it actually reaches (not all upfront), checks eligibility, attempts `list_candidate()` on success, and moves to the next pool candidate on any failure instead of stopping — until `auto_list_top_n` real listings succeed or the pool is exhausted. The old eager Pass-2 verification block in `main()` was removed entirely (verification now happens inside `run_auto_listing()`).
    - **Verified with an isolated monkeypatched test** (no live browser/network/eBay/WooCommerce calls): (a) 10 fake candidates, first 2 fail, next 5 succeed — confirmed the loop stops exactly at the 7th attempt once `top_n=5` successes are reached, candidates 8-10 never touched; (b) 10 fake candidates, first 6 fail, only 4 possible successes remain — confirmed graceful pool exhaustion, exactly 4 listed, no crash, no overrun past `pool_size`.
    - **Cost shape unchanged for good days**: a day where the original top-5 all succeed costs exactly what it always did — extra work only shows up on days with failures, bounded by `replenish_buffer`.
    - Not yet exercised against a real live run as of 2026-08-31 — first real test is the next `--stage finish` run.

37. **Item 37 — step 5 (`branch11_dsers_mapping.py`) has been searching DSers by the wrong field, so it has never successfully found an imported item.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed)`. Found live while answering Travis's question about whether a DSers import links to AliExpress automatically (it doesn't; that's what `dsers_sku_remap` is for). **Confirmed root cause, live-tested against the real DSers MCP:** the script called `dsers_my_products(store_id, keyword=<our internal SKU>)`. Tested that exact call directly — 0 results — even though the item was genuinely present in DSers. DSers's keyword search matches on product **title**, not our SKU.
    - **Fix**: `branch11_dsers_mapping.py` now searches by `entry["ebay_title"]` instead of `sku`, with an exact case-insensitive title-match safety check (ambiguous results recorded as `dsers_mapping_error`, never guessed via `results[0]`). `_eligible()` also now requires `ebay_title` present.
    - **Validated live** first via an ad-hoc one-item test (`branch11-388183263465`, the manually-added "Wallet Tracker" listing) — found it, previewed at confidence 75, applied, confirmed `supplier` flipped to `aliexpress` with the correct cost/URL. Then folded into the script and **verified for real**: `--dry-run` against the live ledger found all 9 previously-stuck entries at confidence 75 each (turns out they'd already been sitting imported in DSers this whole time, just invisible to the old SKU search); ran for real immediately after — all 9 mapped successfully, `dsers_mapped: true` written back to the ledger. This step, dormant since it shipped 2026-08-30, is now actually working.

38. **Item 38 — required-aspect resolution missed easy, unambiguous cases where the correct value never appears as an exact literal phrase.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed)`. Found live reviewing 2026-08-31's `AUTO_LIST_FAILED: Could not confidently resolve a required item aspect` cases: category 40101's ("Other Sleeping Aids") required `Type` aspect only offers two values, `"Anti-Snoring Wearable"` / `"Sleep Aid Aromatherapy"` — every mouth-tape candidate is obviously the former, but `_resolve_aspect_value()`'s literal-phrase tier requires the exact eBay-offered string to appear verbatim, which it never does. **Fix:** added `_ASPECT_VALUE_SYNONYMS`, a small hand-curated keyword set per exact (aspect name, eBay value) pair: `("Type", "Anti-Snoring Wearable") -> {"snor"}`, `("Type", "Sleep Aid Aromatherapy") -> {"aromatherapy", "essential oil", "diffuser"}`. Only fires for exact curated pairs; if more than one value matches by synonym, treated as ambiguous, not guessed. **Verified live**: all 3 of that day's aspect-resolution failures now correctly resolve to `{"Brand": ["Unbranded"], "Type": ["Anti-Snoring Wearable"]}`. Re-attempting the actual listing for those 3 then surfaced Item 35's unrelated Medical Devices policy block — so none of those 3 ended up actually publishing that day, but the aspect-resolution mechanism itself is confirmed working for future candidates in this category not also caught by Item 35.

39. **Item 39 — exclusion list for whole product categories, checked as early as possible in the pipeline.** `DONE (2026-08-31, via chat, Travis-directed)`. Travis's explicit ask: cut off excluded items as early as makes sense so no downstream work (DSers search, judging, browser verification, listing attempts) gets wasted on them; first entry is the Item 35 eBay Medical Devices block. **Implemented** in `branch11_pipeline.py`: `EXCLUDED_CATEGORIES` (a list of `(reason, keyword-set)` pairs, same curated/explainable style as `_ACCESSORY_WORDS`/`_BRAND_TOKENS`) checked via `_excluded_reason(title)` inside `_scrape_ebay_tiles()` — the earliest possible point, right when a raw eBay tile is scraped, before it's even an `EbayCandidate`. Shared by both `--stage discover` and the old `--stage full` fallback. First entry: `{"mouth tape", "anti-snoring", "anti snoring", "snore", "snoring"}`. **Verified against real data**: re-ran the filter against all 59 of 2026-08-31's discovered items — **36 of 59 (61%)** would have been excluded, confirming mouth-tape/anti-snoring items were dominating that day's discovery pool far more than realized. All 4 previously-confirmed Medical-Devices-blocked items were correctly caught; spot-checked the rest for false positives, none found.
    - **Follow-up same day**: of the 23 non-excluded items, only 2 more (beyond the already-listed hamster cage) were HIGH-verdict/profit-passing — PawHut Cat Litter Box and Portable Metal Small Dog Playpen, both `NOT_LISTED` simply because they fell outside that day's top-5-by-profit verification cutoff, not because of any error. Verified and attempted both: **Portable Metal Small Dog Playpen — LISTED** (SKU `branch11-203094541405`, listingId `800593037042`, mirrored to WooCommerce product id 39). **PawHut Cat Litter Box — turned out to already be live**, listed back on 2026-08-28 (listingId `800576598448`, still `ACTIVE`) — resurfaced in discovery as a recurring Deals-page item; `create_offer` correctly rejected the duplicate attempt, its `listing_status` was corrected from a stale `NOT_LISTED`. **Process note**: this attempt hand-picked candidates rather than calling `select_auto_list_candidates()` (which already dedupes against the ledger) — no harm done since eBay's own offer-uniqueness constraint caught it, but any future ad-hoc re-listing pass should call that function rather than hand-picking.

43. **Item 43 — discovery-stage tile scraping never scrolled, so `MAX_TILES_PER_URL` was never actually reached; daily good-item volume was falling short on days whose 2 rotated URLs happened to be thin/expensive categories.** `DONE (2026-09-02, via chat, Travis-directed)`. Travis's ask: restore a "keep working through today's 2 rotated URLs in batches until enough good items are found" loop, extending hard caps if needed, with the good-item target configurable. **Live-tested first, before writing any code**: navigated directly to that day's 2 real rotated URLs (`yard-garden-outdoor-living`, `car-accessories`). Found `_scrape_tiles_for_url()`/`EBAY_TILE_JS` only ever query the DOM once right after `browser_navigate()` — no scroll, ever. `yard-garden-outdoor-living` rendered 24 tiles on initial load but **lazy-loaded to 72 tiles (3x) after one scroll-to-bottom**; `car-accessories` stayed flat at 8 either way (genuinely thin category, not a scroll issue). Also confirmed `?_pgn=2`-style pagination does **not** work on these curated Deals pages (same 24 tiles came back) — a dead end, unlike the keyword-source pages' real paginatable eBay search. Combined with the $100 `--price-ceiling` (yard-garden's scrolled 70 tiles were 91% over $100 — only 6 passed), today's real shortfall was ~60% scroll-blindness + ~90% category-shape; the price ceiling itself is a separate, larger lever left untouched pending a future decision.
    - **Implemented in `branch11_pipeline.py`**: new `_scroll_to_load_more(count_js)` scrolls to the bottom, waits `SCROLL_SETTLE_SECONDS` (1.5s), and checks a cheap tile-count-only JS (`EBAY_TILE_COUNT_JS`/`EBAY_SEARCH_TILE_COUNT_JS`), repeating up to `MAX_SCROLL_ATTEMPTS` (4) or until the count stops growing — a no-op cost on thin pages like car-accessories that don't lazy-load anything further. Wired into `_scrape_tiles_for_url()` for both the plain-Deals-page path and the keyword-source search path. `MAX_TILES_PER_URL` raised 50 → 100 alongside this, now that it can actually be reached (real observed max was ~70-72) instead of being permanently inert.
    - **Also discovered the "batch until N good items, then stop" loop Travis was picturing already exists**, just not documented in memory: Item 27 (`branch11_dsers_search.py`'s early-stop, 2026-09-01) already processes discovered eBay items in order and stops calling `dsers_find_product` once `auto_list_top_n + replenish_buffer` eligible (HIGH-verdict, profit-passing) candidates are found — scoped exactly to whatever today's 2 rotated URLs produced. That loop was starved, not missing: with only 8 raw items some days, it never got close to its target. The scroll fix directly feeds it more supply to work through.
    - **Made the good-item target configurable, per Travis's explicit ask**: `branch11_daily_run.sh` now sets `TARGET_GOOD_ITEMS="${BRANCH11_TARGET_GOOD_ITEMS:-5}"` and `REPLENISH_BUFFER="${BRANCH11_REPLENISH_BUFFER:-5}"` at the top, and passes them explicitly to both step 2 (`--early-stop-pool-size $((TARGET_GOOD_ITEMS + REPLENISH_BUFFER))`) and step 3 (`--auto-list-top-n`/`--auto-list-replenish-buffer`) — one place to change "5" without touching `branch11_pipeline.py` source (the old `AUTO_LIST_TOP_N_DEFAULT`/`AUTO_LIST_REPLENISH_BUFFER_DEFAULT` constants still exist and still govern any ad-hoc/manual run that doesn't pass these flags).
    - **Verified live**: re-ran `discover_pipeline()` directly (read-only, no file writes, confirmed today's real `branch11_ebay_only_2026-09-02.json` byte-unchanged afterward) against today's real 2 rotated URLs post-fix — raw candidates went **8 → 13** (matches the live per-page numbers exactly: 1→6 passing yard-garden, 7 unchanged on car-accessories), took ~100s (acceptable for a daily cron). `bash -n`/`py_compile` clean. **First full real cron run (2026-09-03, 8am) surfaced a real, unrelated production break — see Item 44.**

44. **Item 44 — 2026-09-03's 8am `branch11-daily-item-research` cron (the first full run since Item 43) failed: DSers's OAuth token endpoint returned a transient 503, and the MCP SDK's refresh-failure handling had no retry, falling straight through to a full interactive OAuth flow that a headless cron can never complete.** `DONE (2026-09-03, cron-driven production-readiness run)`. Same underlying fragility class as Item 34 (DSers OAuth), but a genuinely new failure mode, not a repeat of that bug. **Diagnosed from the real run history** (`openclaw cron runs --id 83dfa147-ff0f-42fc-9466-b289e33a6053`), not assumed: discovery succeeded (`branch11_ebay_only_2026-09-03.json`, 12 raw candidates — see the note below on what this means for Item 33), then `branch11_dsers_search.py` crashed. Full traceback showed the real chain: `_handle_refresh_response()` in the vendored `mcp` SDK (`mcp/client/auth/oauth2.py`) treats *any* non-200 refresh response as fatal — it discards the (perfectly good) refresh token, the next request goes out unauthenticated, draws a 401, and the SDK falls into a full interactive authorization-code flow (`=== DSers one-time authorization required ===`, browser + `localhost:8734` callback). Under an unattended cron with no human present, `CallbackServer.wait_for_callback()` just burns its full 600s timeout and the whole run dies (`command exited with code 1`) — for what was really a momentary blip on DSers's token endpoint (a bare `503`), not an actually-invalid token.
    - **Fix, in `branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py`'s existing `_RefreshAwareOAuthClientProvider`** (the same subclass Item 34 already added to work around a different gap in the same base class — a natural place for this, not a new pattern): new `_handle_refresh_response()` override retries the refresh POST directly (rebuilding the request via `self._refresh_token()`, sending it with a short-lived `httpx2.AsyncClient`) up to `_REFRESH_RETRY_ATTEMPTS` (3 total attempts) with linear backoff (`_REFRESH_RETRY_DELAY_S` × attempt, i.e. 3s then 6s) whenever the response is a 5xx, before delegating to the base class's real handling. A genuine 4xx (an actually-invalid/revoked token) is never retried — falls through to the base class immediately, same as before, so this doesn't mask a real auth failure, only a transient server-side one.
    - **Verified**: (a) an isolated mocked test confirmed the retry fires and keeps retrying across the attempt budget on repeated 503s until a 200 succeeds, and confirmed zero retries happen on a 401 (a real auth failure, not transient) — both cases pass; (b) a real live auth-only session (`dsers_session()` → `list_tools()`) against the real DSers MCP server succeeded cleanly post-fix (13 tools), confirming the credential path itself still works end-to-end (DSers's token endpoint had recovered by the time of this check, so the retry branch's *live* trigger condition wasn't re-observed today, but the isolated test directly exercises that exact code path). `py_compile` clean.
    - **Real-world impact of the incident**: 2026-09-03's 8am run produced zero candidates, zero email report, and zero DSers-mapping pass. **Recovered via a real catch-up run of `branch11_daily_run.sh`** (two attempts needed — the first was killed by a session/turn boundary before completing, unrelated to the fix; the second ran to completion): all 5 steps succeeded end-to-end under the exact real-world conditions that broke yesterday — 12 candidates processed, 1 real item auto-listed (`branch11-385826161635`, listingId `800606889105`, mirrored to WooCommerce product id 42), report email sent to Travis, DSers-mapping pass completed cleanly (0 newly mapped, 1 pending Travis's WooCommerce-import click, expected/normal). This is a genuine live confirmation of the fix, not just the isolated test — the DSers OAuth session authenticated and stayed authenticated through a real multi-step run with zero interactive-flow errors.
    - **Bears directly on Item 33**: today's real discovery count was 12 raw candidates (`branch11_ebay_only_2026-09-03.json`) — the first real post-Item-43 number, now available. Comfortably under the 200-raw-tile ceiling Item 33 is tracking; see that item's write-up for the updated recommendation.

- 2026-09-12 (cron-driven production-readiness run): implemented — found and fixed a real bug (Item 0's write-up) where 3 of 4 Branch 11 crons' registered `--command` strings had a literal embedded newline splitting `flock -n <lockfile>` from the script path, silently defeating each cron's self-overlap lock on every run (confirmed via `stderr: flock: bad file descriptor` in 10/11 retained `branch11-price-check` runs, reproduced live, root-caused via raw `payload.argv`). Fixed all 3 (`branch11-price-check`, `branch11-production-readiness` itself, `branch11-dsers-mapping`) via `openclaw cron edit --command`, verified each via `cron get` immediately after. Pure infra fix, no Hard Constraint touched.

- 2026-09-06 (cron-driven production-readiness run): Item 33 — caught another real `branch11-listing-rules.md` change (mtime moved again, +139 bytes). Travis reverted `ROTATION_PAGES_PER_DAY_DEFAULT` from 3 back to 2 same day, confirmed live in code ("other pipeline improvements were already getting more items listed, so no need for wider daily discovery too"). This defuses my own last update's urgency: the 143-candidate/22-min day that prompted a "decide soon" recommendation happened under the now-reverted 3-page config and isn't a live risk under the current 2-page/21-URL setup. Real ceiling is back to 200 raw tiles/day. Updated Item 33 to reflect this — no 2-page data point exists yet with the current full 21-URL pool, so recommending Travis wait for the next real 8am run under the reinstated config before finalizing a `--limit` number, rather than react to a data point from a config that no longer exists. `branch11-dsers-mapping` still `ok`.

- 2026-09-06 (cron-driven production-readiness run): Item 33 — today's 8am run is the first real data point under the new 21-URL/3-page discovery config, and it's significant: 143 raw candidates (new high, 48% of the 300-tile ceiling, more than double yesterday's 68), and total run time jumped to ~22 min (vs ~9 min yesterday) — a real, worse-than-linear cost increase traced to DSers-search needing 117/143 items before early-stop found enough eligible candidates (vs only 23/68 yesterday), i.e. a lower match-rate URL mix, not just more raw tiles. This is the actual unbounded-cost scenario Item 33 exists to guard against, now observed for real rather than hypothetical. Updated the item's recommendation to a concrete 150-200 `--limit` range and suggested Travis decide soon given today's real evidence. - 2026-09-03 (cron-driven production-readiness run): Item 45 (new, URGENT) — `openclaw cron list` showed `branch11-dsers-mapping` at `error (4x)`, all "cron: job execution timed out" at exactly the 300s configured timeout. Ran the wrapper directly under `bash -x`: hangs on `Token refresh failed: 400` then falls into the un-completable interactive OAuth flow (same failure shape as Item 44, but this time a real 4xx, correctly not retried). Made one direct diagnostic POST to DSers's token endpoint (read-only, no retry loop) and got the precise error: `invalid_grant`, "DSers refresh_token no longer valid" — DSers's own server confirming the stored refresh token is genuinely dead, not a transient blip. Found and fixed a real, previously-unknown architectural gap regardless of exact cause: `FileTokenStorage` (shared by 6+ DSers-using scripts across multiple crons) read the token file once at construction and clobbered concurrent writes with a stale in-memory snapshot on every save — exactly the mechanism that would produce "already rotated" after today's unusually heavy concurrent DSers usage (Item 44's diagnosis work + two catch-up attempts + a live verification, stacked on the regular 30-min cron). Reworked it to read-fresh/write-under-lock; verified with an isolated test that a concurrent unrelated write no longer clobbers a rotated token. **This does not fix today's already-dead token** — that needs Travis to complete a fresh one-time interactive DSers OAuth login (the URL any DSers script prints on next run). Until then, `branch11-dsers-mapping` will keep failing harmlessly every 30 min, and tomorrow's 8am run will fail partway through (discovery still works, DSers search onward won't). Elevated to top of Tier 1 given this blocks automation outright, not just caps it.

Diagnosed for real from the run's full diagnostics rather than guessing: discovery succeeded (12 raw candidates, `branch11_ebay_only_2026-09-03.json`), then `branch11_dsers_search.py` crashed — DSers's token endpoint returned a bare 503 on a refresh attempt, and the vendored MCP SDK treats any non-200 refresh as fatal, discarding the good refresh token and falling into a full interactive OAuth flow that a headless cron can't complete (600s timeout, `command exited with code 1`). Fixed in `branch11_dsers_mcp_client.py`'s existing `_RefreshAwareOAuthClientProvider` (same subclass Item 34 used for a different gap): new `_handle_refresh_response()` override retries a 5xx refresh response up to 3 total attempts with backoff before falling through to the base class; a real 4xx still isn't retried. Verified with an isolated mocked test (retries correctly on repeated 503s, does not retry on 401) and a real live DSers auth session (13 tools, session established cleanly post-fix). Updated Item 33 with today's real 12-candidate discovery count (comfortably under the 200-tile ceiling, in line with 2026-09-02's 13). Real findings: the `--limit` CLI flag already exists and is fully wired to `discover_pipeline()` (confirmed in code) — `branch11_daily_run.sh` just never passes a value, so this was never actually an engineering gap. More importantly, traced `discover_pipeline()`/`_select_daily_urls()` directly and confirmed the mechanism Item 33 originally worried about (pool growth silently increasing daily volume) doesn't exist in the pipeline that actually runs day to day — `discover_pipeline()` deliberately drops the old pool-expansion fallback, and `_select_daily_urls()` always returns exactly `pages_per_day` URLs regardless of pool size. Real per-day ceiling is already fixed at `pages_per_day(2) × MAX_TILES_PER_URL(50) = 100` raw tiles, insensitive to `DEALS_URL_POOL` growth. Pulled real discovery-stage counts across all available days: only 2026-09-01 (25 items) cleanly reflects the current live architecture, comfortably under the ceiling. Rewrote the item with this grounding and narrowed the ask to a concrete yes/no-plus-number for Travis (e.g. `--limit 60` as a costless defensive cap, or leave as-is) rather than the original open-ended "what number." Still `NEEDS DECISION` — the actual choice is Travis's, but it's now a one-line change away either way.
